A native-container hash, if used, is a separate identity. |
| Certificates/inventory (§§12.1–12.3) | Closure-keyed device-region records; current text does not explicitly delimit native inventory. | Insert the shared scoping rule in §12.3, cross-reference from §9.2, and require complete device-owner/link-proof coverage per root. Multiple device roots can share static closures without merging root-relative contexts. |
| Launch/entry (§10) | bennett_run(program, cc_entry, args, entropy) starts device CC; it is not native main. | Publish a host call/return shim and container/root selection/feature negotiation. Define native argument/result marshalling, including ptr (whose host binding §10 does not specify), status/fault propagation, suspend/reverse/close and ownership. Do not invent a current general launch shim. |
| Budgets (§5.4; §§10–11) | Validated token tuples and identity scoped to a program/run; action_call has no primary token (§4.7). | Bind selected device invocation budgets and token equality to the launch request; charge actual device/replay work. State host/shim accounting separately; a native process has no invented global B. |
| Handles (§3.3; §10; dialect §6.6) | Register ids refer to the loaded program's ledger; run ownership and token validity are explicit. | Map host-visible opaque handles to that program/ledger with validity and cross-launch ownership checks; preserve class vectors and token identities. Do not serialize native pointers as device ids or token tuples as identities. |
| Effect tape (§7.3; §10) | Run-associated R effects and explicit copied hostcall requests; no implicit log of native host I/O. | Associate each device run and tape with the launched root and define host effect/proxy ordering and replay at the boundary, preserving §7.4 reversal. Saved-journal interchange needs its own versioned replay profile. |
| Host memory by reference (§§7.1–7.2; dialect §§8.5, 17.1, 19.4–19.5) | Device layout plus dialect snapshot/replay and boundary-address/promotion rules; copied hostcall bytes do not provide general shared pointers. | Publish the mapping and lifetime protocol named in the statement, including allocator metadata/history and pointer-rich layouts, so ordinary host memory can be supplied safely without universal host promotion. Retain existing snapshot/replay as a construction, not the sole eventual capability. |

For example native main can call marked roots A and B in distinct ISA objects.
Each has its own object/root certificate and root context []; neither
certificate inventories main or host-only libc. A shared linked device
callee is checked in each root's actual invocation tree. A native comparison
executes the corresponding materialised region/callee closure under its
recorded layout, input and tool configuration (dialect §§17.7, 19.5, 19.9).

These notes specify boundary scope and exact remaining contract work; they
implement no container, host shim or memory registration. The existing
bennett-4ht issue tracks that publication/implementation obligation. Current
device contracts and their complete estimate/proof requirements remain in
force; an unimplemented boundary is not evidence that mixed execution works.


# The document under review (round 1 snapshot)

# brt-vbi — delivery of arbitrary-width runtime helper bodies

Council proposal, not a specification or runtime implementation. Origin:
`brt-vbi` (blocked decision), blocking `brt-zct` in bennett-runtimes.
References: [dialect 0.2.12](../../../../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md)
§§12.1, 12.7–12.8, 13.1, 19.1, and the corresponding
[0.3 draft](../../../../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md).

## Exact defect and North Star scope

§12.7 names width-specific ordinary runtime bodies for wide multiply,
divide, remainder and shifts. §12.8 similarly names integer intrinsic
helpers, including slot-based wide forms. §19.1 requires matching-major
runtime bitcode before materialisation. §13.1 publishes an offline
instance-request protocol for arith, but none of these clauses publishes
a runtime-integer request/response contract or a collection procedure for
helpers introduced during normalisation. A consumer cannot depend on
the runtime producer's private generator interface.

This is a **dialect delivery/interoperability gap**, alongside an actual
missing-implementation obligation. It is not a new ISA arithmetic
instruction or launch ABI gap. Naming an ordinary helper already makes
its body required; a finite default library failing to contain i257
does not make that LLVM width unsupported. The gap matters to North Star
§§3.2–3.3, 5.1's whole materialised LLVM and reproducible comparison.
It does not justify narrowing widths, trapping valid arithmetic, omitting
libc/Julia/SQLite closure, or declaring the broader brt-zct work complete.

## Alternatives and concrete witness

Read-only source witness:
`../bennett-runtimes/.tmp/sol-runtimes-queue/tmp/runtime-width-request.ll`.
It loads two i257 values, multiplies, calls `llvm.ctpop.i257`, stores i257.
Both LLVM 18 and 23 `opt -passes=verify -disable-output` accept it.
Both live `dist/llvm<M>/bennett-runtimes.bc` lack definitions for
`__bennett_rt_i257_mul` and `__bennett_rt_i257_ctpop`, confirmed with
matching-major `llvm-nm --defined-only`. These are current observations,
not an inference from the producer's progress report.

For a concrete numerical vector, a=2^256+3, b=2. Modulo 2^257 the product
is 6 and its population count is 2. A five-limb input slot has widths
[64,64,64,64,1]; ctpop writes [2,0,0,0,0]. Dropping the top input limb
can be caught separately by ctpop(a)=3. This requires actual ordinary
bodies, not a declaration that merely passes LLVM verification.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Publish an offline request/manifest protocol in the dialect | Producer-independent, analogous to arith; recommended. |
| Prebuild every admitted width and operation | A possible complete distribution, but costly; a common-width subset is only a cache. |
| Publish a separate runtime ABI note | Viable only if versioned and normatively incorporated by the dialect; a producer README alone cannot bind competitors. |
| Add ISA primitives or a private driver-generator API | Unnecessary machine extension or unpublished coupling. |

## Recommended exact dialect/ABI text

Insert the following titled subsection at the end of §12.8 before the
examples, and cross-reference it from §19.1. Mirror it in both tracks.
The executable name below is a proposed public interface, not a claim
that today's runtimes supplies it.

```text
Runtime integer instance delivery v1.

The runtimes distribution MUST supply an offline executable interface:
  bennett-runtimes-instantiate --request REQUEST --bitcode OUTPUT_BC
                             --manifest OUTPUT_JSON
The driver may configure the executable path. Exit zero means both
outputs are complete; nonzero means no output is usable. Generation
must not require network access, a host runtime service or private
consumer knowledge. The distribution MUST identify its version.

REQUEST is UTF-8 JSON with exactly schema, llvm_major, target_triple,
data_layout and entries. schema is "bennett-runtimes-instantiations-1";
llvm_major is the link's integer LLVM major; target_triple and
data_layout are the link's effective LLVM strings. Each entries element
has exactly op (string) and width (integer, not boolean).

The op set is mul, udiv, sdiv, urem, srem, shl, lshr, ashr from §12.7;
and bswap, bitreverse, ctpop, ctlz, cttz, fshl, fshr, sshl_sat,
ushl_sat, smul_fix, umul_fix, smul_fix_sat, umul_fix_sat, sdiv_fix,
udiv_fix, sdiv_fix_sat, udiv_fix_sat, pdep, pext, clmul,
smul_with_overflow and umul_with_overflow from §12.8. Dots in an
intrinsic name become underscores. width is the original integer
operand width W, not a limb width or result aggregate size. The
operation and width must be admitted by the link's LLVM major and the
corresponding published row; §12.7 requests require W>128, and the
overflow-helper requests require W>64. Unknown fields/operations,
nonintegral/out-of-range widths and target/major/layout mismatches are
errors. Every valid request remains an implementation obligation.

Duplicates denote one instance. Canonical request bytes are UTF-8 JCS,
without a trailing LF, with unique entries sorted by op's unsigned
UTF-8 bytes then ascending numeric width. request_sha256 is the
lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 of those bytes. A supplied width list
or prebuilt cache MUST NOT become a capability limit.

OUTPUT_BC is ordinary matching-major bitcode whose triple and
DataLayout equal the request. For each requested (op,W), provide the
exact public definition, type, scalar flags, result convention and
fault behavior of §§12.7–12.8. In particular, wide mul is
void @__bennett_rt_i<W>_mul(ptr dst,ptr a,ptr b), and wide ctpop is
void @__bennett_rt_i<W>_ctpop(ptr dst,ptr a). The existing shadow-stack
slot layout, partial top limb and push/store/call/load/pop order are
unchanged. All reachable implementation bodies must be supplied or
resolved through the published matching-major base runtime/arith
distributions and their published instance protocols. No unresolved
instance placeholder, trap stub or hidden host implementation counts
as delivery.

OUTPUT_JSON is JCS with exactly schema, request_sha256,
bitcode_sha256 and exports. schema is "bennett-runtimes-instances-1";
bitcode_sha256 hashes the exact OUTPUT_BC bytes. exports lists every
externally visible definition in that module, sorted by unsigned UTF-8
symbol bytes, each with exactly symbol and function_type; function_type
is LLVM's printed function type for the requested major, without the
function name or attributes. Every requested public symbol must occur
exactly once and have its published type. Generation with identical
canonical request and producer version must reproduce both output
byte strings regardless of output paths. The driver verifies hashes,
triple, layout, definitions and types before using the output.

Collection is a link-preparation fixed point. Starting from the selected
device entries and their conservatively reachable bitcode closure,
include calls already naming these helpers and all helpers that the
published normalisation rules will introduce, including vector
scalarisation, N27 and the N22 applications after N27/N32. Collect
arith instances by §13.1 as well. Resolve runtime/arith requests and
their dependencies until no new (provider,instance) is required and
every required body is linked. Include all possible device call
targets; do not select just one runtime input path. Declarations alone
do not satisfy the fixed point. A provider cycle must resolve to actual
linked definitions; repeated unresolved requests are a loud failure.

This discovery is a non-emitting analysis using the published rules;
it does not rerun or change §12.1's normative rule order. Link the
complete collected bodies before final materialisation, identically
in the lifted and native comparison inputs. If later optimisation or
materialisation reveals a new required helper, restart preparation
with the enlarged request and rebuild both outputs; never patch only
one build or silently leave it unresolved. Failure to generate a
valid instance is an implementation blocker, not an unsupported-width
verdict, narrower substitute or permitted fault of the device program.
```

Add to §19.1 after the bitcode-source paragraph:

```text
Before final materialisation the driver MUST complete the runtime
integer instance-delivery fixed point of §12.8 and the arith requests
of §13.1, using the same resolved closure in both builds. Whole-program
device mode uses its entire device closure; selective-module mode
collects the union required by its marked device roots. Neither mode
requires host-only code to become a device region.
```

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

Request and output hashes are different identities; neither is an ISA
`object_hash` or the arith/ref catalog hash. Provider-version changes
invalidate the generation cache even when the request is identical.
Generated bitcode changes materialised closures, costs, stack use and
the resulting object/schedule/certificate bindings. Drivers, plugins,+runtimes, numerical providers and conformance owners must coordinate
publication and repin. No ISA `ref@0.9` recipe change is necessary.

## Conformance vectors and actual verification

The bounded command in [INDEX](INDEX.md) ran both major-specific LLVM
verifiers and symbol audits: exit 0, both missing-definition assertions
passed. This establishes the delivery witness, not helper implementation
or whole-program execution. No sibling gate was run.

After publication require: i129/i192/i257 partial-top-limb values;
mul and ctpop together; large admitted widths with a resource-bounded
generation test; narrow intrinsic helpers; scalar flags and overflow
aggregate types; all helper families with published fault behavior.
Check reordered/duplicate requests canonicalise identically, unknown
op/invalid width/major/layout reject loudly, manifests cannot omit or
mis-type a requested definition, and changed request/bitcode hashes fail.
Test a helper introduced only by scalarisation, one after N32/repeated
N22, a transitive dependency and a discovery restart. Verify byte
reproduction in relocated directories and link/execute both materialised
builds. Merely passing opt verification is insufficient.

## K entry and publication route

**No ISA 0.9/0.10 K entry:** this is a dialect/tool-distribution contract.
Proposed dialect decision row: `RUNTIME-INSTANCES-1 | §§12.8,19.1 |
offline request/manifest and complete device-closure fixed point |
brt-vbi | additive versioned delivery protocol`. Recommend the next
published 0.2 patch and the corresponding 0.3 draft text. A separate
ABI note is acceptable only with a normative versioned dialect reference.
Leave brt-vbi/brt-zct open for ratification and implementation; publication
of a proposal does not unblock a consumer's unpublished interface.

## Mixed fat object

[fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md) binds this
proposal (North Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). Collect helpers for marked modules and
their linked device closures. Multiple roots may share generated bodies;
each device root retains its own certificate/context association. The
ordinary native host binary, host entry/argv/envp and host-only libc stay
outside that inventory. Native comparison binds the matching materialised
device closure. Request manifests are build inputs, not a new launch,
budget, handle or effect-tape protocol. Container/root association and
host-memory mapping still need the exact mixed-launch additions named in
the shared scope note; this delivery decision keeps them reachable.

codex
I’ll check the proposal against both dialect tracks, the cited ISA clauses, and the runtime witness, then grade the diff. I’m using the repository’s beads skill for the required workflow context.

---
name: beads
description: Use when working in a repository that uses bd or Beads for durable project task tracking, issue dependencies, blocker management, multi-session handoff, or shared work memory. Trigger when the user asks to find ready work, claim or close tasks, create follow-up work, inspect blockers, recover project context, or choose between local planning and persistent project tracking.
---

# Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:4796:| N9 | `atomic` and `volatile` on loads and stores; `fence`; `atomicrmw`; `cmpxchg` | plain load/store; fence deleted; `atomicrmw op ptr p, T v` → `%nz.l.r = load T, ptr p`, `%nz.v.r = <op> T %nz.l.r, v` for `add`/`sub`/`and`/`or`/`xor` (for `xchg`: v; `min`/`max`/`umin`/`umax`: `icmp` + `select` named `%nz.k.r`, `%nz.v.r`; FP `fadd`/`fsub`: ordinary `fadd`/`fsub` then N32; `fmax`/`fmin`, `fmaximum`/`fminimum`, `fmaximumnum`/`fminimumnum`: respectively `llvm.maxnum`/`llvm.minnum`, `llvm.maximum`/`llvm.minimum`, `llvm.maximumnum`/`llvm.minimumnum` then N32; `nand`: `%nz.a.r = and T %nz.l.r, v`, then `%nz.v.r = xor T %nz.a.r, -1` (these two logical instructions, in this order, without flags); `uinc_wrap`: k=`icmp uge old,v`, i=`add old,1`, new=`select k,0,i`; `udec_wrap`: k=`icmp eq old,0`, g=`icmp ugt old,v`, w=`or k,g`, d=`sub old,1`, new=`select w,v,d`; `usub_cond`/`usub_sat`: k=`icmp uge old,v`, s=`sub old,v`, new=`select k,s,old` / `select k,s,0`. Names are `%nz.{k,i,v}.r`, `%nz.{k,g,w,d,v}.r` and `%nz.{k,s,v}.r` respectively, all arithmetic at T without overflow flags), `store T %nz.v.r, ptr p`, uses of `%r` → `%nz.l.r`; `cmpxchg ptr p, T c, T n` → `%nz.l.r = load`, `%nz.e.r = icmp eq T %nz.l.r, c`, `%nz.s.r = select i1 %nz.e.r, T n, T %nz.l.r`, `store T %nz.s.r, ptr p`, `%nz.a.r = insertvalue { T, i1 } zeroinitializer, T %nz.l.r, 0`, `%r = insertvalue { T, i1 } %nz.a.r, i1 %nz.e.r, 1` (single-thread assumption, recorded in the certificate, §24 D24). Volatile accesses in platform-profile code are first routed by N35 (§20.3) |

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:4863:**`n-defined-zero`.** N12 totalises the flags before N27. This does not exempt abs, ctlz or cttz from expansion: full materialised output contains no calls to these LLVM intrinsics, including false-flag calls. At x=0 both counts are 8, so the sum is 16; at x=1 they are 7 and 0, so the sum is 7.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6818:## 13. Floating point (arith contract)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6824:### 13.1 Soft-float rewrite (rules N19, N32)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6863:For each such name fmt and each B of §13.1, N32 MUST emit `iN @__bennett_sf_convert_to_<fmt>_B(C_B, i32 R, i1 Sat)` for to-arbitrary (format MDString removed, rounding encoded by §13.5, immediate saturation retained), and `C_B @__bennett_sf_convert_from_<fmt>_B(iN)` for from-arbitrary; fmt is spelled exactly as its case-sensitive MDString, N is its listed bit width, and results are integer bits / bitcast B respectively. Finite conversions use the named format's rounding, saturation and nonfinite rules with canonical NaNs; N1 defines a source-poison conversion result as zero bits, including unsaturated out-of-range FP4/FP6 inputs. A missing APFloat lowering is an implementation obligation, never permission to reject a valid named format or emit an unresolved numerical placeholder.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6865:**Static instantiation request v1.** The driver MUST collect required concrete arith instances before the final link and supply a UTF-8 JSON object with exactly `schema: "bennett-arith-instantiations-1"`, `llvm_major` (integer), `target_triple` (string), `data_layout` (LLVM string), and `entries` (array); a conversion entry has exactly `op: "convert_to" | "convert_from"`, `format` (the MDString above), and `base` (B suffix), and a powi entry has exactly `op: "powi"`, `base`, and `exponent_bits` (integer K, 1..8388608). A powi entry requests both `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>(C_B, iK)` and `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>_rm(C_B, iK, i32 R)` (the suffix `_rm` is last); a conversion entry requests its exact signature above. Duplicate entries denote one instance; canonical request bytes are JCS with entries sorted by (op, base, format or empty string, exponent_bits or zero).
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6871:N32 emits `void @__bennett_sf_<op>_B_wide(ptr d, C operand, i32 M)`.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6875:N22 loads each limb at width min(64,M−64k). For sitofp/uitofp from iM, store
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6922:### 13.2 Vector floating point
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6928:### 13.3 Formats beyond binary32 and binary64
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6933:### 13.4 Transcendentals and libm intrinsics (N33)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6984:### 13.5 Constrained intrinsics, `strictfp` and the floating-point environment
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:7000:**Quiet and signaling comparison ABI.** N32 MUST lower `llvm.experimental.constrained.fcmp` to `i1 @__bennett_sf_cmp_B_rm(C_B, C_B, i32 P, i32 R)` and `llvm.experimental.constrained.fcmps` to the distinct `i1 @__bennett_sf_cmps_B_rm(C_B, C_B, i32 P, i32 R)`, with P exactly the 0..15 predicate encoding of §13.1 and R = 0 (these intrinsics have exception metadata but no rounding metadata); vector forms scalarise first, and no predicate-bit extension encodes signalingness.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4775:| N9 | `atomic` and `volatile` on loads and stores; `fence`; `atomicrmw`; `cmpxchg` | plain load/store; fence deleted; `atomicrmw op ptr p, T v` → `%nz.l.r = load T, ptr p`, `%nz.v.r = <op> T %nz.l.r, v` for `add`/`sub`/`and`/`or`/`xor` (for `xchg`: v; `min`/`max`/`umin`/`umax`: `icmp` + `select` named `%nz.k.r`, `%nz.v.r`; FP `fadd`/`fsub`: ordinary `fadd`/`fsub` then N32; `fmax`/`fmin`, `fmaximum`/`fminimum`, `fmaximumnum`/`fminimumnum`: respectively `llvm.maxnum`/`llvm.minnum`, `llvm.maximum`/`llvm.minimum`, `llvm.maximumnum`/`llvm.minimumnum` then N32; `nand`: `%nz.a.r = and T %nz.l.r, v`, then `%nz.v.r = xor T %nz.a.r, -1` (these two logical instructions, in this order, without flags); `uinc_wrap`: k=`icmp uge old,v`, i=`add old,1`, new=`select k,0,i`; `udec_wrap`: k=`icmp eq old,0`, g=`icmp ugt old,v`, w=`or k,g`, d=`sub old,1`, new=`select w,v,d`; `usub_cond`/`usub_sat`: k=`icmp uge old,v`, s=`sub old,v`, new=`select k,s,old` / `select k,s,0`. Names are `%nz.{k,i,v}.r`, `%nz.{k,g,w,d,v}.r` and `%nz.{k,s,v}.r` respectively, all arithmetic at T without overflow flags), `store T %nz.v.r, ptr p`, uses of `%r` → `%nz.l.r`; `cmpxchg ptr p, T c, T n` → `%nz.l.r = load`, `%nz.e.r = icmp eq T %nz.l.r, c`, `%nz.s.r = select i1 %nz.e.r, T n, T %nz.l.r`, `store T %nz.s.r, ptr p`, `%nz.a.r = insertvalue { T, i1 } zeroinitializer, T %nz.l.r, 0`, `%r = insertvalue { T, i1 } %nz.a.r, i1 %nz.e.r, 1` (single-thread assumption, recorded in the certificate, §24 D24). Volatile accesses in platform-profile code are first routed by N35 (§20.3) |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4790:| N17 | (0.1: FP formats and transcendentals → F(8)) | replaced by §13 (N32, N33): no floating-point construct faults |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4792:| N19 | floating-point arithmetic, comparison and conversion of `float` and `double` | the soft-float rewrite of §13.1 (applied by N32) |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4795:| N22 | **wide integers and non-byte memory**: an integer type iN with N > 128 in an instruction; a load or store of iN with N not a multiple of 8 or of an aggregate type | iN with N > 128 becomes L = ⌈N/64⌉ i64 limbs (limb 0 the least significant; the top limb is i(N − 64(L−1))) with the expansions of §12.7; a load or store of iN with N mod 8 ≠ 0 becomes a load or store of i(8⌈N/8⌉) with a `trunc` after the load and a `zext` before the store (fixing LangRef's unspecified extra bits as zero); a load or store of an aggregate becomes one access per element in element order at its offset (`%nz.e<k>.<r>`) |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4800:| N27 | **integer intrinsics** | the fixed expansions of §12.8 |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4805:| N32 | **floating point** | the soft-float rewrite of §13.1–§13.2 (after N21) |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4842:**`n-defined-zero`.** N12 totalises the flags before N27. This does not exempt abs, ctlz or cttz from expansion: full materialised output contains no calls to these LLVM intrinsics, including false-flag calls. At x=0 both counts are 8, so the sum is 16; at x=1 they are 7 and 0, so the sum is 7.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6797:## 13. Floating point (arith contract)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6803:### 13.1 Soft-float rewrite (rules N19, N32)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6842:For each such name fmt and each B of §13.1, N32 MUST emit `iN @__bennett_sf_convert_to_<fmt>_B(C_B, i32 R, i1 Sat)` for to-arbitrary (format MDString removed, rounding encoded by §13.5, immediate saturation retained), and `C_B @__bennett_sf_convert_from_<fmt>_B(iN)` for from-arbitrary; fmt is spelled exactly as its case-sensitive MDString, N is its listed bit width, and results are integer bits / bitcast B respectively. Finite conversions use the named format's rounding, saturation and nonfinite rules with canonical NaNs; N1 defines a source-poison conversion result as zero bits, including unsaturated out-of-range FP4/FP6 inputs. A missing APFloat lowering is an implementation obligation, never permission to reject a valid named format or emit an unresolved numerical placeholder.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6844:**Static instantiation request v1.** The driver MUST collect required concrete arith instances before the final link and supply a UTF-8 JSON object with exactly `schema: "bennett-arith-instantiations-1"`, `llvm_major` (integer), `target_triple` (string), `data_layout` (LLVM string), and `entries` (array); a conversion entry has exactly `op: "convert_to" | "convert_from"`, `format` (the MDString above), and `base` (B suffix), and a powi entry has exactly `op: "powi"`, `base`, and `exponent_bits` (integer K, 1..8388608). A powi entry requests both `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>(C_B, iK)` and `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>_rm(C_B, iK, i32 R)` (the suffix `_rm` is last); a conversion entry requests its exact signature above. Duplicate entries denote one instance; canonical request bytes are JCS with entries sorted by (op, base, format or empty string, exponent_bits or zero).
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6850:N32 emits `void @__bennett_sf_<op>_B_wide(ptr d, C operand, i32 M)`.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6854:N22 loads each limb at width min(64,M−64k). For sitofp/uitofp from iM, store

 succeeded in 0ms:
proves pointer-free (no slot holds a pointer atom, §5), the pointee's byte range as an
(offset, length) pair and the bytes themselves. The host-side stub calls the function on a copy,
writes back the bytes of pointer arguments, and replies with the result and the written bytes.
A pointer argument whose pointee may contain pointers read by device code does not become a host
call: the callee is **promoted** to a CC variant (§19.4; ISA-11 would let it stay native, an
efficiency item). Every service is declared in `devices.services` (ISA §9.1, §10).

### 11.5 Fault codes reported from CC

ISA 0.8 does not specify which code a CC trap reports. `__bennett_nz_fault(C)` in
CC lowers to `hostcall bennett.fault` with a payload holding C, then `trap`; the service
records C in the run's fault statistics. A host call is forbidden in R and a generic R trap
cannot preserve arbitrary C. Typed R faults therefore have dependency blocker BLK-5 =
ISA-7 (§25.1).

On ISA 0.8 a module has verdict blocked:BD-B-ISA7 if its device program contains
an R-tier variant reached as an R entry: a reversible region candidate invoked outside
generation, or the §19.2 whole-program R entry. Every such variant has the §17.6 budget
check, regardless of supplied run-time limits. A reversible call treated as a direct call
under §7.4 is not an R entry and does not by itself block. Other rejection/blocker
precedence remains §1.6. The R blocker trap is generic ISA trap with no hostcall, reached
only on executions that would report a typed fault (budget exhaustion, F(C), nz_fault(C));
all other executions are unaffected. This fallback does not implement typed reporting.

 Required operation: R fault C
terminates with fault record code C, performs no subsequent source effect and preserves
reversible prefix history. No ISA 0.8 generic-trap path is claimed to implement this contract.

### 11.6 Examples

The R-tier console: a whole program in the reversible tier writes three bytes and returns 0;
the tape holds one `console.write`; whole-run reversal restores the entry state:

```llvm example=effect-console-r verdict=blocked:BD-B-ISA7 rmode=@main
@msg = private unnamed_addr constant [3 x i8] c"hi\0A"

define i32 @main() {
  call void @__bennett_effect_console_write(ptr @msg, i64 3)
  ret i32 0
}
```

```text blocker=effect-console-r
BD-B-ISA7 R entry: BLK-5 (ISA-7); mandatory pre-instruction typed fault 1 requires ISA 0.9 K5 fault C, exact terminal code, no subsequent effects, preserved reversible prefix
```

```text cc-run=effect-console-r
driver: --bennett-tier=reversible --bennett-entry=main
run: status=COMPLETED results=[INT(32):0] faults=[]
tape: console write args=[A(@msg), 3] bytes=68690a
reverse: status=REVERSED console=empty memory=entry
```

The same marker in a CC device function is a host effect:

```llvm example=effect-console-cc verdict=accept
@msg = private unnamed_addr constant [3 x i8] c"ok\0A"

define void @report() {
  %h = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 1)
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h)
  call void @__bennett_effect_console_write(ptr @msg, i64 3)
  ret void
}
```

```text cc-run=effect-console-cc
run: status=COMPLETED results=[] faults=[]
hostcall: service=bennett.dev.console.write payload=6f6b0a reply=empty
```

The ENTROPY-bound oracle of rung 8 is §16.8.

## 12. Materialised normalisation (norm 0.2.8)

### 12.1 Scope, order and naming

The normaliser rewrites every device variant (§4.3) and the initialisers of every global a
device variant references; H variants are untouched. It applies the rules of §12.2 in table
order, each to the whole module before the next, with one exception: N33 is applied immediately
before N32 so any floating-point instructions introduced by its expansions are processed
by N32; own-format carrier calls and bit-exact reconstruction remain as specified in §13.4. After N27 and N32, re-apply N22
to every instruction they created with an integer side wider than 128 bits. Only
width legalisation repeats; no other rule re-runs. Width lowering may be fused
with an expansion when the resulting canonical limb rows are identical. The result is the **materialised module**: the
reference semantics (§18), the lifting map (§16) and the native comparison build (§17.7) are all
defined on it (NS §3.2: "the native comparison binary is built from that materialised bitcode").

**Naming.** A rule that introduces a value names it `%nz.<tag>.<r>`, where `<r>` is the name of
the instruction being rewritten without its `%` (a number for an unnamed value). For a rewritten
instruction without a result (a `void` call, a `store`, an `invoke` of a `void` function), `<r>`
is `<f>.<k>`: f the enclosing function's name and k the 0-based count, in instruction order, of
the result-less instructions of f that the same rule rewrites. A value introduced once per
function is `%nz.<tag>.<f>`. A constant global a rule needs is `@nz.<tag>.<f>.<r>`. A
declaration a rule needs is appended at the end of the module, and a global after the module's
globals, in order of first use. Each rule
states its tags.

### 12.2 The rules

**Fault replacement F(C)** (§12.3) and the 35 rules. N13, N17, N18 and N20 are 0.1 ids whose
rules are replaced (outline §12.2); they are kept as anchors.

| Id | Source | Replacement |
|---|---|---|
| N0 | the intrinsic families that LLVM defines by its own lowering passes: `llvm.coro.*`; `llvm.matrix.*`; `llvm.type.test`, `llvm.public.type.test`, `llvm.type.checked.load`, `llvm.type.checked.load.relative`, `llvm.icall.branch.funnel`; `llvm.instrprof.*`; `llvm.alloc.token.id`; `llvm.experimental.guard`, `llvm.experimental.widenable.condition` | the normaliser runs, only when the module contains one of them, the passes of the major in use, in this order: `coro-early`, `cgscc(coro-split)`, `coro-cleanup`; `lower-matrix-intrinsics`; `lowertypetests`; `instrprof`; `alloc-token`; `lower-guard-intrinsic`; `lower-widenable-condition` (which gives `true`). | N26 | **memory intrinsics**: `llvm.memcpy.*`, `llvm.memcpy.inline.*`, `llvm.memmove.*`, `llvm.memset.*`, `llvm.memset.inline.*`, `llvm.memcpy/memmove/memset.element.unordered.atomic.*`, `llvm.experimental.memset.pattern.*` | `call void @__bennett_rt_memmove(ptr d, ptr s, i64 n)`, `call void @__bennett_rt_memset(ptr d, i8 v, i64 n)` (the `i1` volatile flag and element-size operand dropped; a constant length becomes an `i64` constant; a narrower non-constant length is first `%nz.n.<r> = zext … to i64`); `memset.pattern(d, v: T, count)` → a loop storing v at d + i·size(T) for i < count (blocks `nz.mp.<r>.*`) |

| N35 | **platform hooks** | §20.3 (MMIO routing), §20.4 (interrupt poll points), only under a platform profile |

The other generic intrinsics are rows of §12.8 (integer), §13 (floating point), §14 (control
transfer) and §15 (target intrinsics). **Completeness obligation:** Appendix C lists every
generic (target-independent) intrinsic name of LLVM 18, 20 and 23 (546 names) with its rule. No
generic intrinsic is `trapped`.

*(non-normative)* Division by zero and `sdiv`/`srem` of INT_MIN by −1 are not rewritten: they are
faults of the reference semantics (§17.5), the ISA's `lift.div` and CC division fault on them
directly (ISA §4.1, §4.2), and the native comparison build traps (§17.7).

# spec/

`Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md` is draft **0.2.12** (2026-11-01), from eight consumer-named gaps,
awaiting council review. It supersedes accepted draft 0.2.11 (round 10, sol/codex);
accepted 0.2.10 is retained in git, with binding interpretations in
[`errata-0.2.10.md`](errata-0.2.10.md). Binding interpretations of 0.2.12 itself (text and SHA
unchanged) are in [`errata-0.2.12.md`](errata-0.2.12.md). The reviewer disposition table is in
[`../docs/briefs/2026-11-01-dialect-0212-consumer-gaps.md`](../docs/briefs/2026-11-01-dialect-0212-consumer-gaps.md).
It targets
ISA 0.8 (`../bennett-isa-spec/spec/`, settled) and is graded against it. `bennett_markers.h` is the
normative C/C++ header of the marker ABI (section 3.3). `Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.2.PROGRESS.md` is
the writing job's log. Council records and the runner live in the `bennett` meta repo
(`docs/council/`, `tools/council.py`); the rubric is `docs/council/rubric-isa.md`, under which any
narrowing of north-star scope is S0.

Rules for changing it:

- **No narrowing** (CLAUDE.md rule 10). A compile-time rejection needs a row in section 22.1 citing
  the north-star clause or maintainer decision that requires it. A deferral (section 24) postpones
  mechanics only and states the capability it keeps. A capability ISA 0.8 cannot express is a
  blocker (section 25.1) with the exact ISA change and a `blocked:` verdict, never a rejection.
- Every semantic change bumps the full version string (header and section 23); drafts are not
  compatible with each other. The reference objects and hashes of section 16 depend on the ISA
  version string, the lifting map and the layout, so they change with any of them.
- **Checked examples** (section 1.5, process rules P-1 to P-3):
  - P-1: every rule id has an `accept` example, every rejection a `reject` example, every blocker
    a `blocked` example; Appendix B is the index, and a rule without one fails the check.
  - P-2: an example the checker cannot yet execute fails (`checker-unimplemented: <capability>`);
    no example is removed or weakened to make the checker pass; the freeze gate is all green.
  - P-3: executed results come from independent executors (LLVM tools, native binaries, the Rust
    ISA reference interpreter `bennett-isa-ref`); Python computes expected artefacts only.
- Run `python3 scripts/dialect_check_v02.py` before anyone grades this draft; it must exit 0. (For 0.2 the
  checker is being extended against the Rust interpreter in a separate job; until then the 0.2
  examples were executed by the writing job as its progress file records.)
- Add every new example or stated result as a tagged block, so the checker executes it; an
  untagged `llvm` or `isa` block is an error. A `semantics=` example must be byte-equal to its
  `corpus/legality/cases/spec-*.ll` file; run `python3 corpus/legality/harness/run.py baseline
  --majors 18,20,23 --cases 'cases/spec-*'` (headless, CLAUDE.md rule 11) after changing one.
- Write derivations out before stating numbers; every stated example result must be computed.
- Surgical edits only; a rewrite has introduced a mechanical error every time it was tried (ISA
  rounds 1-8).
- Revisions go back through the council.
# Binding interpretations of accepted dialect 0.2.10

Published with draft 0.2.12, 2026-11-01. Accepted text at `023f5a771a814270634db3be9cfde88ded69f975`, spec SHA-256 `d4f3c1a88391cc147f8c8fa4f80f82852e3ebe6f880610fc16cd5151f33d7206`, is unchanged; consumer pins remain valid. These interpretations resolve ambiguity in that text; new 0.2.12 ABIs/accuracy requirements are identified separately and are not retroactive ABI changes.

## mkb — §§5.2, 7.2, 12.2 N0

**N0 and resolution order.** Before testing the forwarding-thunk predicate or freezing candidates, the plugin MUST apply the N0 passes of §12.2 to the virtual device bodies used by S2; §7.2 resolution then uses those bodies, and §7.3 hashes their materialised closures.

The plugin MUST retain A(S) for every original source function or alias whose address reaches the region operand, dispatching that address to its post-N0 resolved candidate even when N0 removes the forwarding call; a declaration remaining after N0 is still F(8), but an intrinsic call removed by N0 MUST NOT turn its containing defined body into an external candidate.

## jjr — §§16.9, 17.1

The thin malloc body in §16.9 is a specialised witness whose calls all request 16 bytes. ISA allocation itself is exactly byte-bumping, not aligned; C malloc still owes its target ABI alignment. The general wrapper rounds its requested extent and inserts explicit charged padding allocations when an intervening runtime primitive leaves the next address unaligned, identically in both builds. This resolves the example-versus-C-ABI ambiguity without altering ISA §7.1.

## lbq — §§11.2–11.3, 14.7, 19.2

**Console syscall framing.** For virtual stdout/stderr, runtimes MUST implement `writev` by validating the vector count and total length before the first effect, then emitting one `console.write` per nonempty vector in source order (zero-length writes emit no record), returning the total byte count; the descriptor ABI limits are 0..1024 vectors and total at most target SSIZE_MAX, with invalid counts/overflow returning EINVAL and a null nonempty vector table returning EFAULT.

The console shim MUST return ENOTTY for `ioctl(TIOCGWINSZ)`, ESPIPE for seeking, and success for closing a virtual stdout/stderr descriptor, with no extra device effect or terminal metadata; this close releases no host resource and does not disable the virtual byte sink.

**Model history.** R console writes MUST use only the ISA effect tape, while L model state is ordinary reversible memory initialised from descriptor bindings, private and discarding only under the oracle rules of §11.3; action console capacity exhaustion MUST fault before mutation under TDL-12, and a standalone native-model test undo log MUST NOT substitute for either tier's history. No second production model-history ABI is required.

**Process context.** Before entering `<sym>`, the driver/runtimes preparation MUST establish a static single-thread musl process context with the supplied argc/argv/envp bytes, a null-terminated argv and envp, `environ`, program name, initial fenv and the libc/stdio state needed by the linked program; comparison-only Linux `_start` is permitted, but Linux ELF crt/TLS/auxv entry is not required in device tiers.

For a C `main` entry, the runtimes entry wrapper MUST treat normal return as `exit(status)`, running registered exit handlers and flushing stdio exactly once under §14.7 before the launch stub catches the exit pseudo-exception; explicit `exit` follows that same path, and `_Exit` bypasses handlers/flush as §14.7 specifies.

## 0na — §§3.1, 17.7, 19.5

The “nobody: primitives” ownership row applies to production bitcode before ISA lowering, not native comparison support implementing those primitives after materialisation. Such support must preserve device layout and history. Draft 0.2.12 §19.5 publishes the new v1 layout transport ABI; that spelling is not imposed retroactively on 0.2.10. The existing memory/fault outcomes and North Star definedness classifiers are not waived by UBSan-only support.

## dfd — §§13.1, 13.5

The explicit exact-conversion and correct-rounding guarantees take priority over APFloat implementation fallbacks. PPCDoubleDouble denotes high+low, not the legacy consecutive-106-bit approximation. A low component may not be discarded when the exact sum is representable in the destination. Sparse-tail loss and intermediate unsigned-low saturation are not permitted by the named-format reference. Draft 0.2.12 §13.1 makes canonical-pair rounding and flags explicit; no claim that the current arith publication already meets these guarantees is made.

## 1av — §§13.4–13.5

“No correct-rounding claim” does not authorise an arbitrary deterministic non-numerical substitute for the named operation or waive the specified IEEE flags. The accepted text does not quantify its numerical error. The one-local-ulp carrier bound and precise constrained accuracy/flag policy in draft 0.2.12 are NEW requirements, not retroactive assertions that 0.2.10 contained a numeric bound or that measured sweeps prove a full-domain bound.

## z7u — §13.1 arbitrary-format row

“Format named by metadata” means the LLVM major’s named interpretation MDString and its format/carrier semantics, not an unbounded user-created APFloat precision/exponent schema. LLVM 23 Intrinsics.td and LangRef define to(value, format, rounding, saturation) and from(bits, format). Draft 0.2.12 publishes NEW concrete carrier operand spellings and a static request/manifest protocol; those new ABI details are not claimed to have existed in 0.2.10.

## 21a — §§13.1, 13.5

The generic “soft-float call of §13.1 with suffix _rm” does not merge the distinct LLVM quiet fcmp and signaling fcmps operations. Quiet-NaN invalid behavior must remain distinguishable. Draft 0.2.12 publishes the NEW cmps_B_rm spelling and exact operand/exception lowering in §13.5; the absence of an earlier spelling was a contract gap, not authority to silently substitute quiet comparison.
# Binding interpretations of dialect 0.2.12

Published 2026-11-02. Text of draft 0.2.12 at `c775e6d458006fde143f2bf8ec62f07b034fb431`, spec SHA-256 `cfcce70e84ead8a05924c0921f13fc9cb477fdd71f5448c7f6f357ea91349e83`, is unchanged; the full version string, map 0.2.9 and consumer pins remain valid. These interpretations clarify that text without changing any reference result. Draft 0.3 carries the same text in §§5.1, 5.2 and 24.

## 5u3 — §§5.1, 5.2, 24 (council run `contract-5u3-2026-11-02`, round 1 sol and codex accept; `accept-4ht-recheck-2026-11-02` round 1 PASS; muse accept in `accept-muse-batch-2026-11-02`; bead `b-dialect-spec-5u3`)

**§5.1, after K.** The reference bounds INT atoms as above; it imposes no additional
cardinality bound on pointer-offset sets or memory intervals. Implementers
MAY use exact symbolic sets, shared interval relations and SCC
acceleration instead of enumerating represented offsets or slots.
All reference sets and byte/representation-width distinctions must be
preserved for verdicts, candidate sets and dispatch domains. A coarser
summary is not the reference result merely because it is sound for
aliasing. An unfinished analysis reports an unfinished build with the
rule and exhausted implementation resource, not a source rejection,
completed verdict or run-time fault for a valid input.

**§5.2, after the least-fixpoint paragraph.** In a pure modular-translation inclusion SCC, if every cycle transfer is
an unfiltered fixed displacement at one bit width W and preserves one
base/provenance coordinate, close the generated displacements as a
subgroup of Z/(2^W). For a single self-cycle of stride s, each seed a
denotes the coset a modulo gcd(s,2^W). Union distinct seed cosets. For
multiple vertices, retain vertex-specific path displacements; do not
merge their seed coordinates. For edges u→v labelled d_uv,
choose root-to-vertex path displacements p_v and let
g=gcd(2^W,{p_u+d_uv−p_v}). A seed a at u contributes
{a−p_u+p_v+kg mod 2^W} at v. Union contributions from all seeds.
Apply the same rule to a memory relation
only when every transfer preserves the complete storage interval and
representation width and all PT6–PT8 read/overlap results are identical
to the represented individual slots. Retain every ROOT and FN alternative
explicitly. Otherwise use the original rules or another exact method.
PT2's pointer-index width and preserved high pointer bits still apply;
this rule does not replace them by unrestricted full-pointer addition.

**§24.** Analysis representation/acceleration is implementation policy; the
default result is the exact §5 least fixpoint, including existing K=4096
INT abstraction. Time or memory caps do not alter that result. A report
of unfinished analysis keeps the remaining implementation obligation open.

## 19. The driver (driver 0.2.1)

### 19.1 Pipeline position and bitcode sources

Front ends emit bitcode with their stock pre-link pipelines; no Bennett code runs before the final
link (Enzyme PR #3211: "forgetting it is a loud failure"), except in the explicit closed-unit mode
(§19.7). The driver links all bitcode inputs (§19.8), links `bennett-runtimes` and `bennett-arith`
bitcode of the link's major M, runs the post-link pipeline (`lto<O3>` by default, or any envelope
pipeline of §21.1 the user selects), then runs the plugin on the whole program (NS §3.3). With
`opt`:

```
opt -load-pass-plugin=libbennett-plugin-<M>.so -passes='lto<O3>,bennett' -bennett-dialect=0.2.12 …
```

The plugin runs on one merged module (full LTO; §24 D23). It registers one module pass, `bennett`,
and MAY register a full-LTO last extension point for use from a linker plugin; it MUST do nothing
in a pre-link phase. **Bitcode sources** (NS §3.3): Rust `std` from the bitcode embedded in rlibs
(`.llvmbc`) where present and readable by §19.8, else `-Zbuild-std`; the Julia runtime by the
runtimes' shim bitcode; every remaining call without bitcode is a `trapped` coverage line (§4.5).

### 19.2 Mode `--bennett-tier=reversible --bennett-entry=<sym>`

The whole program is the device program in tier R (NS §4.2: "entered through
`__bennett_reversible` or a driver mode"; rungs 3, 5, 6):

- a CC **launch stub** fetches the argv and envp bytes with `hostcall bennett.argv`, initialises
  the shadow stack (§17.3), calls the R entry `<sym>` with (argc, argv, envp), catches the exit
  pseudo-exception (§14.7) and returns the status;
- effects are the ISA device models (§11.2): console, clock, entropy (the launch's entropy tape),
  block with `block_image` from `--bennett-disk=<file>`;
- un-run is `bennett_reverse` (ISA §10): whole-run reversal restores the entry state; tape replay is
  deterministic from the tape and the entropy;
- a `measure` reachable in the program is `BD-E-REVERSIBLE-MEASURE` (§7.5b).

**Process context.** Before entering `<sym>`, the driver/runtimes preparation MUST establish a static single-thread musl process context with the supplied argc/argv/envp bytes, a null-terminated argv and envp, `environ`, program name, initial fenv and the libc/stdio state needed by the linked program; comparison-only Linux `_start` is permitted, but Linux ELF crt/TLS/auxv entry is not required in device tiers.

For a C `main` entry, the runtimes entry wrapper MUST treat normal return as `exit(status)`, running registered exit handlers and flushing stdio exactly once under §14.7 before the launch stub catches the exit pseudo-exception; explicit `exit` follows that same path, and `_Exit` bypasses handlers/flush as §14.7 specifies.

### 19.3 Mode `--bennett-oracle=<sym>` ("oracle from program")

A convenience: the driver synthesises the translation unit a quantum programmer would write
(§16.7): a CC function that builds a descriptor binding `<sym>`'s parameters by
`--bennett-bind=<param>:<kind>:<width>[:<value>]` (kind `in`, `arg`, `mem_in`, `out`), device
bindings by `--bennett-dev=<model>:<width>[:<value>]`, and a payload: the return value, the exit
status caught by the wrapper (§14.7), or the first L bytes of console output
(`--bennett-payload=return|exit|console:<L>`), and applies the oracle once to registers it
allocates, as a device entry the host calls with those registers. The normative route is the
separate translation unit with marker calls naming the unmodified function (rungs 4, 7, 8).

### 19.4 Placement: host vs device CC

H functions stay native unless they share **pointer-rich** memory with device code: a pointer
argument of a device-entry call, or a global accessed by both, whose pointee may contain pointers
that device code reads (§5). Such H functions are **promoted** to CC variants, transitively up to
their roots (in the limit `main`, and then H is the system-call proxy only: `hostcall
bennett.syscall`). Results are equal by the materialised semantics; ISA-11 would let them stay
native (an efficiency and placement item, §25.3; outline E11).

### 19.5 Layout: the comparison link

The driver links the materialised module natively (§17.7) with a linker script that fixes the
addresses: non-PIE; identical-code folding and constant merging off; the null region [0,
`null_size`) with `null_size` = 4096; the data sections (`.rodata`, `.data`, `.bss`, in that order)
from `data_base` = 4096; the text from `text_base` = 0x400000, or, when the device ranges below
(data, heap, arena, interface regions, computed first) end above 0x400000, from the first multiple
of 0x200000 at or after their end, so that the native comparison binary maps its code disjointly
from them; the defaults are options (`--bennett-layout-data-base`, `--bennett-layout-text-base`,
§24). The link map gives A(F) for
every function and the base of every global. The device `layout` (ISA §9.1) replicates it:

- `globals`: every global of the link at its link address with its image;
- `heap_base`: the first multiple of 4096 at or after the end of the data; `heap_limit_max`: the
  largest `heap` of the budget table (which covers the shadow stack, §9.2);
- the arena (TDL-4) at the first multiple of its size 2^A at or after the heap's end, with 2^A the
  smallest power of two covering the may-Co globals and the coherent allocator's heap (its size
  from `--bennett-arena-heap`, default the largest `heap` of the table); the arena's shadow
  `__bennett_rt_shadow` is a global of the data sections;
- interface regions per oracle above the arena (§16.3); H, A_tab and S_cells by §16.3;
- the production host image is linked with the same addresses for every function and global
  whose address may cross the boundary (§4.4).

*(non-normative)* With data low and text high, H covers the data, heap and regions; function
addresses are values only and never occupy device memory.

**Native layout ABI v1.** The driver MUST define exactly one external constant `@__bennett_rt_native_layout = constant { i64, i64, i64 } { i64 1, i64 heap_base, i64 heap_limit_max }, align 8` in comparison-only support, with fields at byte offsets 0, 8 and 16 in target little-endian order and values from this same resolved §19.5 layout; runtimes declares that object and validates the version/range before use, with no weak/default definition. The third field is a byte capacity, not an end address; it includes shadow-stack allocations under §9.2. This object has no relationship to blockaddress identity objects `__bennett_rt_blk.<f>`.

### 19.6 Build-time snapshots

`--bennett-snapshot=<dump>` rewrites the initialisers of the mutable globals listed in the dump
(and the platform RAM global, §20.1) to the dump's bytes before the plugin runs; the data images
then carry the snapshot and R of §8.5 is empty for them (rung 7: "kernel state snapshot fixed
classically after boot"). The dump is recorded by hash in the build record.

### 19.7 Closed-unit mode

`-bennett-closed-unit` (the REPL fast path, NS §3.3: "a per-TU invocation remains available as a
fast path for programs closed within one unit, with the same semantics"): the plugin runs on one
module plus the runtimes and arith bitcode, after a **closedness check**: every device function's
callees are defined in that set or are trapped exactly as in whole-program mode; no device
function's address escapes the module; no function pointer enters through a device-entry
parameter. If the check passes, processing is identical to whole-program mode. If it fails, the
driver falls back to whole-program mode (it does not reject). Objects are content-addressed (ISA
§9.2) and linked at launch (ISA §9.3), so the REPL is incremental.

Closed-unit mode MUST retain whole-program semantics after its closedness check and fall back to whole-program processing when that check fails. [deps: DLT-19.1, DLT-19.7, ISA-9.2, ISA-9.3]

### 19.8 Mixed LLVM majors

The driver reads every bitcode input with the newest major M of the envelope (bitcode is readable
by newer majors), links with M and runs plugin M; runtimes and arith ship bitcode for each M.
Every input MUST have one target datalayout and triple (NS §3.2) (`BD-E-VERSION` otherwise: a
tool-version mismatch, not a program property). An input newer than M is `BD-E-VERSION`. The
tested (front end, major) pairs are published in the envelope; a corpus case links clang 18
bitcode with Julia's and rustc's majors (§21.2; bd `.16`).

Mixed-major bitcode links within the tested envelope MUST use its newest reader and matching plugin and library bitcode, retaining one target triple and DataLayout. [deps: DLT-19.8, DLT-21.1, ISA-9.1, ISA-9.2]

### 19.9 Options, identity, outputs, module flag

| Option | Meaning | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `-bennett-dialect=<v>` | MUST equal the plugin's dialect version (`BD-E-VERSION`) | required |
| `-bennett-isa=<v>` | MUST equal the ISA version the plugin targets (`BD-E-VERSION`) | `0.8` |
| `--bennett-budgets=<file>`, `--bennett-budget-default=<s,h,st,d,ss>` | §9.2 | §9.2 defaults |
| `--bennett-class=<name>` | §3.4 | none |
| `--bennett-tier=reversible`, `--bennett-entry=<sym>`, `--bennett-disk=<file>` | §19.2 | – |
| `--bennett-oracle=<sym>`, `--bennett-bind=…`, `--bennett-dev=…`, `--bennett-payload=…` | §19.3 | – |
| `--bennett-snapshot=<dump>` | §19.6 | – |
| `-bennett-closed-unit` | §19.7 | off |
| `--bennett-platform=<profile>` | §20 | none |
| `--bennett-vscale=<V>`, `--bennett-cpu=<model>` | §12.5, §15.2 | §24 |
| `--bennett-qreg-instances=<n>` | §6.5 | 64 |
| `-bennett-inplace-exhaustive-bits=<n>` | §8.7 | 24 |
| `-bennett-warn-qstore` | §6.7 | off |
| `-bennett-out=<dir>` | the outputs below | required |

**Identity.** `llvmGetPassPluginInfo` returns plugin name `bennett` and version string
`dialect-0.2.12;isa-0.8`; the driver refuses a plugin whose string differs. [deps: DLT-19.9, DLT-23] One plugin build per LLVM
major (NS §3.2).

**Outputs** (in `-bennett-out`, file names plugin-defined, §24 D22): the materialised device
variants as bitcode (§17.7); the host module with device entries replaced by launch stubs; the ISA
objects (ISA §9, §16); the certificate (ISA §12) with `producer.materialisation = {llvm_version,
pipeline, normaliser_version: "0.2.8"}` and `bindings.dialect_mapping_version = "0.2.9"`; the coverage
report (ISA §13), whose `source` field is `dialect-0.2.12:<rule id>` (e.g. `dialect-0.2.12:N33`); the
blocker reports (§25.1); and the **build record**, one JCS object with the build flags, the
budget table (resolved, including each shape's selection_identity, final semantic identity,
resolved components and frozen layout capacities under §9.2) and its SHA-256, the runtimes, arith and asm-table hashes, the snapshot
hash, the layout options and the `cpuid` leaf table (§15.2). Its SHA-256 is recorded as the build metadata of the certificate's
`producer.version` (`<driver version>+build.<hex>`) until the ISA certificate has a field for it
(§25.2). [deps: DLT-19.9, DLT-25.2]

**A missing plugin fails loud.** No production artefact defines a marker: `bennett-runtimes`,
`bennett-arith` and the driver's link inputs define no `__bennett_*` marker symbol (test runtimes
such as the trace runtime do, and are never linked into production). A link in which the plugin
did not run therefore leaves undefined references and fails at link time; the driver MUST NOT
ignore unresolved symbols or supply weak marker definitions.

**Module flag.** A producer MAY add `!{i32 1, !"bennett.dialect", !"0.2.12"}` (behaviour Error, so an
LTO link of modules of two dialect versions fails); the plugin MUST reject a module whose flag
differs from its version (`BD-E-VERSION`). [deps: DLT-19.9, DLT-23]

```llvm example=version-flag verdict=reject:BD-E-VERSION
define void @top() {
  %h = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 1)
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h)
  ret void
}

!llvm.module.flags = !{!0}
!0 = !{i32 1, !"bennett.dialect", !"0.1"}
```

### 13.1 Soft-float rewrite (rules N19, N32)

**Formats and carriers:**

| LLVM type | Suffix | Carrier |
|---|---|---|
| `half` | `f16` | `i16` |
| `bfloat` | `bf16` | `i16` |
| `float` | `f32` | `i32` |
| `double` | `f64` | `i64` |
| `x86_fp80` | `f80` | `i80` |
| `fp128` | `f128` | `i128` |
| `ppc_fp128` | `ppcf128` | `i128` |

The rewrite of an instruction `%r` with floating-point operands or result is: first, for each
floating-point operand k (value or constant, in operand order), `%nz.<k>.r = bitcast <T>
<operand> to <carrier>`; then the call below, named `%nz.i.r`; then, for a floating-point result,
`%r = bitcast <carrier> %nz.i.r to <T>`. The entry points are defined by `bennett-arith` and are
part of its bitcode the driver links. B below is the format's suffix, C its carrier.

| Instruction | Call (carrier operands in order) | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `fadd`, `fsub`, `fmul`, `fdiv`, `frem` | `C @__bennett_sf_{add,sub,mul,div,rem}_B(C, C)` | bitcast |
| `fcmp <p>` | `i1 @__bennett_sf_cmp_B(C, C, i32 P)`, P the LLVM predicate number: `false` 0, `oeq` 1, `ogt` 2, `oge` 3, `olt` 4, `ole` 5, `one` 6, `ord` 7, `uno` 8, `ueq` 9, `ugt` 10, `uge` 11, `ult` 12, `ule` 13, `une` 14, `true` 15 | `%r` is the call itself |
| `fptosi`/`fptoui` to iM, `llvm.fptosi.sat`/`llvm.fptoui.sat` to iM | `i128 @__bennett_sf_{fptosi,fptoui}_B(C, i32 M)` | `%r = trunc i128 %nz.i.r to iM` (the call is `%r` when M = 128) |
| `sitofp`/`uitofp` from iM | `%nz.x.r = sext`/`zext iM a to i128` (omitted when M = 128), `C @__bennett_sf_{sitofp,uitofp}_B(i128)` | bitcast |
| `fpext`, `fptrunc` from format S to format D; `llvm.convert.to.fp16`, `llvm.convert.from.fp16` | `D_carrier @__bennett_sf_{fpext,fptrunc}_S_D(S_carrier)` | bitcast |
| `llvm.fptrunc.round(x, mode)` | `D_carrier @__bennett_sf_fptrunc_S_D_rm(S_carrier, i32 R)`, R from the mode (§13.5) | bitcast |
| `llvm.sqrt`, `llvm.floor`, `llvm.ceil`, `llvm.trunc`, `llvm.round`, `llvm.roundeven`, `llvm.rint`, `llvm.nearbyint`, `llvm.canonicalize` | `C @__bennett_sf_<name>_B(C)` | bitcast |
| `llvm.minnum`, `llvm.maxnum`, `llvm.minimum`, `llvm.maximum`, `llvm.minimumnum`, `llvm.maximumnum` | `C @__bennett_sf_<name>_B(C, C)` | bitcast |
| `llvm.fma` | `C @__bennett_sf_fma_B(C, C, C)` | bitcast |
| `llvm.is.fpclass(x, mask)` | `i1 @__bennett_sf_isfpclass_B(C, i32 mask)` | the call |
| `llvm.ldexp(x, e)`, `llvm.frexp(x)` | `C @__bennett_sf_ldexp_B(C, i32 e)`; `{ C, i32 } @__bennett_sf_frexp_B(C)` | bitcast (for frexp: of field 0) |
| `llvm.lround`, `llvm.llround`, `llvm.lrint`, `llvm.llrint` to iM | `i128 @__bennett_sf_{lround,llround,lrint,llrint}_B(C, i32 M)` | `trunc` to iM |
| `llvm.convert.to.arbitrary.fp`, `llvm.convert.from.arbitrary.fp` (format named by metadata) | `@__bennett_sf_convert_to_<fmt>_B` / `@__bennett_sf_convert_from_<fmt>_B` with LLVM APFloat's semantics of the named format | bitcast |
| `fneg`, `llvm.fabs`, `llvm.copysign`, `bitcast`, `load`, `store`, `select`, `phi` | unchanged: bit-exact operations (LangRef) | – |

**Arbitrary-format naming and carriers.** Format metadata MUST be the LLVM-major LangRef interpretation MDString, not a user-defined precision/exponent record; the integer carrier is exactly the interpretation's storage width, scalarised lane by lane by N21. LLVM 23's names are `Float8E5M2`, `Float8E5M2FNUZ`, `Float8E4M3`, `Float8E4M3FN`, `Float8E4M3FNUZ`, `Float8E4M3B11FNUZ`, `Float8E3M4`, `Float8E8M0FNU` (i8), `Float6E3M2FN`, `Float6E2M3FN` (i6), and `Float4E2M1FN` (i4). These intrinsics are absent on LLVM 18; the name set is the major's published set, not a list of formats that its current APFloat converter happens to implement.

For each such name fmt and each B of §13.1, N32 MUST emit `iN @__bennett_sf_convert_to_<fmt>_B(C_B, i32 R, i1 Sat)` for to-arbitrary (format MDString removed, rounding encoded by §13.5, immediate saturation retained), and `C_B @__bennett_sf_convert_from_<fmt>_B(iN)` for from-arbitrary; fmt is spelled exactly as its case-sensitive MDString, N is its listed bit width, and results are integer bits / bitcast B respectively. Finite conversions use the named format's rounding, saturation and nonfinite rules with canonical NaNs; N1 defines a source-poison conversion result as zero bits, including unsaturated out-of-range FP4/FP6 inputs. A missing APFloat lowering is an implementation obligation, never permission to reject a valid named format or emit an unresolved numerical placeholder.

**Static instantiation request v1.** The driver MUST collect required concrete arith instances before the final link and supply a UTF-8 JSON object with exactly `schema: "bennett-arith-instantiations-1"`, `llvm_major` (integer), `target_triple` (string), `data_layout` (LLVM string), and `entries` (array); a conversion entry has exactly `op: "convert_to" | "convert_from"`, `format` (the MDString above), and `base` (B suffix), and a powi entry has exactly `op: "powi"`, `base`, and `exponent_bits` (integer K, 1..8388608). A powi entry requests both `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>(C_B, iK)` and `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>_rm(C_B, iK, i32 R)` (the suffix `_rm` is last); a conversion entry requests its exact signature above. Duplicate entries denote one instance; canonical request bytes are JCS with entries sorted by (op, base, format or empty string, exponent_bits or zero).

The arith distribution MUST provide a documented offline generator accepting that request and producing matching-major ordinary bitcode plus a JCS manifest containing `schema: "bennett-arith-instances-1"`, the request SHA-256 as `request_sha256`, `bitcode_sha256`, and `exports` (array of objects with exact `symbol` and LLVM `function_type` strings sorted by symbol); every requested definition and its reachable closure MUST be present, with no hardware FP or host libm import, and linked before normalisation identically in both builds. Default prebuilt exponent widths are a cache, never a capability bound; generation failure is an explicit implementation blocker, not a successful link, unsupported-format verdict, or silent narrower substitute.

**Wide integer sides (M>128).** The preceding i128 conversion rows apply only
to M≤128. For fptosi/fptoui, their .sat forms and lround/llround/lrint/llrint,
N32 emits `void @__bennett_sf_<op>_B_wide(ptr d, C operand, i32 M)`.
Here d is a shadow-stack slot of L=ceil(M/64) limbs at byte offsets 8k (§12.7).
The entry writes the exact M-bit saturated result, limb 0 least significant,
using truncation toward zero / the rounding rule above, with NaN mapped to 0;
N22 loads each limb at width min(64,M−64k). For sitofp/uitofp from iM, store
all limbs in slot s and call `C @__bennett_sf_<op>_B_wide(ptr s, i32 M)`,
correctly rounded from the exact signed/unsigned M-bit integer. No i128 carrier
intervenes. For canonical temporary slots, emit ss_push(8L,8), then limb
GEP/store rows in increasing k; for destinations, push then call then limb
GEP/load rows in increasing k; pop slots in reverse push order after their
last use. Bitcast FP operands before destination push and FP results after pop.
The usual shadow-stack checks, budget charges and linked-runtime materialisation
apply. The helper's suffix op is fptosi/fptoui for the corresponding .sat form.

**Semantics of the entry points** (the contract with `bennett-arith`): IEEE 754-2019 for
binary16 (`half`), binary32, binary64 and binary128; `bfloat` is the IEEE-style format with 8
exponent and 7 fraction bits; `x86_fp80` is the 80-bit x87 extended format (64-bit significand
with explicit integer bit; encodings with exponent ≠ 0 and integer bit 0, the unnormals, and
pseudo-denormals and pseudo-infinities are treated as NaN inputs, as modern x87 hardware does);
`ppc_fp128` is double-double with the operations of LLVM's APFloat `PPCDoubleDouble` semantics of
the major in use. For all formats: round to nearest ties to even, subnormals supported, no
observable flags or exceptions (except §13.5); **every NaN result is the format's canonical NaN**
(sign 0, quiet bit set, other payload bits 0: `0x7E00` f16, `0x7FC0` bf16, `0x7FC00000` f32,
`0x7FF8000000000000` f64, `0x7FFFC000000000000000` f80, `0x7FFF8000…0` f128, and for ppcf128
the f64 canonical NaN in the high double and +0 in the low); `rem` is C `fmod` (exact, the sign of
the dividend); `cmp` is LLVM's `fcmp` for the predicate; `fptosi`/`fptoui` and the `.sat` forms
truncate toward zero, map NaN to 0 and saturate values outside the range of iM to its bounds,
returned sign-extended (`fptosi`) or zero-extended (`fptoui`) in i128 when M≤128,
and in the full limb slot when M>128; `sitofp`/`uitofp` round to
nearest even; `minnum`/`maxnum` are IEEE 754-2008 minNum/maxNum (one NaN operand: the other
operand; two: the canonical NaN) with −0 < +0; `minimum`/`maximum` are IEEE 754-2019 (either NaN:
the canonical NaN) with −0 < +0; `minimumnum`/`maximumnum` are IEEE 754-2019 minimumNumber/
maximumNumber; `sqrt`, `fma` and every rounding function are correctly rounded (`round` ties
away from zero, `rint` and `nearbyint` to nearest even); `fpext` is exact; `fptrunc` rounds to
nearest even; `canonicalize` maps every NaN to the canonical NaN and is the identity otherwise;
`is.fpclass` tests LangRef's ten classes on the bit pattern; `ldexp`/`frexp` are exact up to
rounding of a subnormal `ldexp` result; `lround`/`llround` round half away from zero and `lrint`/
`llrint` to nearest even, saturating like `fptosi`. *(non-normative)* `fneg`, `fabs` and
`copysign` stay bit-exact, so a NaN's sign bit set by `fneg` survives, as LangRef requires; an
implementation of `half` and `bfloat` + − × ÷ √ in binary32 followed by one rounding is
correctly rounded, since 24 ≥ 2p + 2 for p = 11 and p = 8 (Figueroa's double-rounding bound), but
the contract is correct rounding, so `fma` needs its own implementation.

**PPCDoubleDouble priority and rounding.** The reference to the LLVM major's APFloat `PPCDoubleDouble` names its two-binary64 value representation and operation conventions, not permission to violate this section's explicit numerical guarantees: implementations MUST preserve the exact real value high + low for conversions and the correctly-rounded requirements for `sqrt`, `fma` and rounding-to-integral operations, and MUST NOT substitute the legacy consecutive-106-bit fallback when it changes such a result.

For these guarantees, a finite canonical double-double is a nonoverlapping pair (h,l) of binary64 values with h = roundTiesToEven(h+l) and l the exact residual (positive zero when the residual is zero), zero represented as (signed zero,+0); the result MUST be the canonical pair whose exact sum is nearest the exact mathematical result (or its directed neighbour for §13.5), with ties to the even low-component significand, using the high significand when the low component is zero, and NaN/inf represented in the high component with low +0. Overflow is determined by rounding with an unbounded high exponent first and then applying binary64's high-component exponent limit with IEEE overflow outcomes for the active mode (the finite maximum or infinity as appropriate); underflow uses tininess after rounding of the exact pair sum, with subnormal threshold 2^-1022.

Cross-format conversion MUST use the exact sum of both components, be exact whenever that sum is representable in the destination, and otherwise round once to the destination format under the requested mode; it MUST NOT drop a representable low component, saturate an in-range positive total because its low component is negative, or introduce intermediate-conversion flags. For example, high = 1 and low = 2^-60 convert to x86_fp80 as exactly 1 + 2^-60, not 1. The `fpext` exactness statement describes true numerical widening; a cross-format pair whose value sets are not nested still uses this conversion rule, regardless of equal storage width.

Constrained operations covered by these guarantees MUST derive invalid/divbyzero/overflow/underflow/inexact from the mathematical operation and final rounding, not the status of an inaccurate APFloat fallback or an intermediate high/low operation; for sqrt/fma/conversion, inexact means the final pair's exact sum differs from the exact finite result, and underflow requires both tininess after rounding and inexactness. Rounding-to-integral follows the named IEEE operation: rint/lrint/llrint signal inexact on a nonintegral finite operand, nearbyint suppresses inexact, and floor/ceil/trunc/round/roundeven/lround/llround do not raise inexact merely for discarding a fractional part. Other PPC operations retain their named major-specific APFloat semantics subject to the explicit canonical-NaN rules above.

### 13.2 Vector floating point

After N21 every vector floating-point operation is scalar: §13.1 applies lane by lane, and
`vector.reduce.fadd`/`fmul` are the ordered left folds of §12.6 (exact once N2 has removed
`reassoc`).

### 12.7 Wide integers (N22)

Let w_k = min(64, N − 64k). Limbs a_k, b_k (k = 0 … L − 1) have type i<w_k>.
Every limb operation, constant and incoming carry uses w_k, with an identity conversion
when widths agree (in particular an i1 carry into an i1 top limb needs no zext):

- `add`: `%nz.m0.r = add a_0, b_0`, `%nz.c0.r = icmp ult %nz.m0.r, a_0`; for k ≥ 1:
  `%nz.t<k>.r = add a_k, b_k`, `%nz.z<k>.r = zext i1 %nz.c<k−1>.r to i<w_k>`, `%nz.m<k>.r = add
  %nz.t<k>.r, %nz.z<k>.r` and, for k < L − 1, `%nz.u<k>.r = icmp ult %nz.t<k>.r, a_k`,
  `%nz.v<k>.r = icmp ult %nz.m<k>.r, %nz.t<k>.r`, `%nz.c<k>.r = or i1 %nz.u<k>.r, %nz.v<k>.r`;
- `sub`: let beta_-1=0. At limb k compute t_k=a_k−b_k modulo 2^w_k,
  z_k=zero-extended beta_(k−1) (identity if w_k=1), and m_k=t_k−z_k modulo
  2^w_k. When another limb follows, beta_k=(icmp ult a_k,b_k) OR
  (icmp ult t_k,z_k). Discard the final outgoing borrow. Canonical names use
  `%nz.m0.r = sub a_0,b_0`, `%nz.c0.r = icmp ult a_0,b_0`; thereafter
  `%nz.t<k>.r`, `%nz.z<k>.r`, `%nz.m<k>.r`, `%nz.u<k>.r`, `%nz.v<k>.r`,
  `%nz.c<k>.r` in that order. In particular v_k tests t_k<z_k, never m_k<t_k.
  This derives wrapping subtraction from [LangRef sub](https://llvm.org/docs/LangRef.html#sub-instruction)
  and [usub.with.overflow](https://llvm.org/docs/LangRef.html#llvm-usub-with-overflow-intrinsics);
- `and`, `or`, `xor`: per limb; `icmp`: lexicographic from the top limb (signed on the top
  limb only for signed predicates); `select`, `phi`: per limb; `zext`, `sext`, `trunc`: limb
  copies; `sext` extracts the sign at bit w_top − 1, sign-extends the old partial top
  limb when widening it, and fills each new limb with that sign at its own width;
- `mul`, `udiv`, `sdiv`, `urem`, `srem`, `shl`, `lshr`, `ashr`: calls of the runtimes entry points
  `__bennett_rt_i<N>_<op>(ptr r, ptr a, ptr b)` on shadow-stack slots holding the limbs (division
  by zero calls `__bennett_nz_fault(5)`, signed MIN / −1 `__bennett_nz_fault(6)`, shifts by ≥ N
  give 0 as N14);
- loads and stores: per limb at offsets 8k, little-endian targets (`%nz.p<k>.r` / `%nz.q<k>.r`
  for the addresses);
- a wide parameter or result of an internal device function becomes L limb parameters or an
  aggregate of limbs.

**Pointer-index width witness.** With I=32 and pw=64, index 2^32 becomes
zero. Adding one to low 0xffffffff wraps to zero without carrying into the
high pointer bits. These materialised pairs are unchanged by normalisation;
the state witness executes their §16.4 mapped objects, and native executes LLVM.

```llvm normalise-in=n-gep-index
target datalayout = "e-p:64:64:64:32"
define i64 @f(i64 %i) {
entry:
  %p = inttoptr i64 4294967296 to ptr
  %q = getelementptr i8, ptr %p, i64 %i
  %r = ptrtoint ptr %q to i64
  ret i64 %r
}
define i64 @g(i64 %base, i64 %i) {
entry:
  %p = inttoptr i64 %base to ptr
  %q = getelementptr i8, ptr %p, i64 %i
  %r = ptrtoint ptr %q to i64
  ret i64 %r
}
define i64 @h() {
entry:
  ret i64 ptrtoint (ptr getelementptr (i8, ptr inttoptr (i64 8589934591 to ptr), i64 1) to i64)
}
```

```llvm normalise-out=n-gep-index

  %p = inttoptr i64 4294967296 to ptr
  %q = getelementptr i8, ptr %p, i64 %i
```
