# Mixed fat object — shared contract scope

Proposal for every revision in this directory; not a published ISA amendment.
Clause references are to [ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md),
[the North Star](../../../../bennett/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md), and, where
qualified, the maintained dialect 0.2/0.3 texts. This is the shared statement
required by bennett-4ht and the council rubric's standing mixed-fat-object check.

## Citable scoping statement

> A mixed fat object retains an ordinary native host binary and embeds ISA
> objects for marked device modules and their linked device closures (North
> Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). ISA §§9.1–9.3 identify each device object and selected
> root; §12.3 certificates, owner records and reports cover that closure,
> with a separate binding/context set per device root and [] relative to
> that root (§12.1). Native entry, argv/envp and host-only libc need no ISA
> region, frame or certificate record; native comparison binds the selected
> materialised closure, not the whole host. Today §10 launches a device CC
> entry with copied arguments/entropy; §5.4 budgets bind device invocations,
> §3.3/§10 handles belong to a loaded program/run, and §7.3/§10 tapes cover
> device R effects. These require explicit container-to-object/root/certificate
> association, host-entry-to-device-entry bindings, budget/handle lifetime
> negotiation and tape/run association in a versioned mixed-launch contract.
> ISA §7.1 defines device memory but §10 defines no general host-memory-by-reference
> launch ABI: that contract must add address/layout mapping, range/liveness,
> aliasing, ownership/synchronisation, writeback and reversal rules, preserving
> dialect §§6.6, 8.5, 19.4–19.5. Until published, these are explicit
> bennett-4ht boundary obligations, not grounds to require whole-binary
> lowering or omit mixed execution.

## Present assumptions and exact publication changes

| Contract object | Assumption today | Exact change needed for mixed use |
|---|---|---|
| Object and linking (§§9.1–9.3) | One ISA object's closed sections and linked symbols; no native-container schema. | Publish a versioned inventory associating embedded/linked ISA object bytes and object_hash with selected device roots and certificates. A native-container hash, if used, is a separate identity. |
| Certificates/inventory (§§12.1–12.3) | Closure-keyed device-region records; current text does not explicitly delimit native inventory. | Insert the shared scoping rule in §12.3, cross-reference from §9.2, and require complete device-owner/link-proof coverage per root. Multiple device roots can share static closures without merging root-relative contexts. |
| Launch/entry (§10) | bennett_run(program, cc_entry, args, entropy) starts device CC; it is not native main. | Publish a host call/return shim and container/root selection/feature negotiation. Define native argument/result marshalling, including ptr (whose host binding §10 does not specify), status/fault propagation, suspend/reverse/close and ownership. Do not invent a current general launch shim. |
| Budgets (§5.4; §§10–11) | Validated token tuples and identity scoped to a program/run; action_call has no primary token (§4.7). | Bind selected device invocation budgets and token equality to the launch request; charge actual device/replay work. State host/shim accounting separately; a native process has no invented global B. |
| Handles (§3.3; §10; dialect §6.6) | Register ids refer to the loaded program's ledger; run ownership and token validity are explicit. | Map host-visible opaque handles to that program/ledger with validity and cross-launch ownership checks; preserve class vectors and token identities. Do not serialize native pointers as device ids or token tuples as identities. |
| Effect tape (§7.3; §10) | Run-associated R effects and explicit copied hostcall requests; no implicit log of native host I/O. | Associate each device run and tape with the launched root and define host effect/proxy ordering and replay at the boundary, preserving §7.4 reversal. Saved-journal interchange needs its own versioned replay profile. |
| Host memory by reference (§§7.1–7.2; dialect §§8.5, 17.1, 19.4–19.5) | Device layout plus dialect snapshot/replay and boundary-address/promotion rules; copied hostcall bytes do not provide general shared pointers. | Publish the mapping and lifetime protocol named in the statement, including allocator metadata/history and pointer-rich layouts, so ordinary host memory can be supplied safely without universal host promotion. Retain existing snapshot/replay as a construction, not the sole eventual capability. |

For example native main can call marked roots A and B in distinct ISA objects.
Each has its own object/root certificate and root context []; neither
certificate inventories main or host-only libc. A shared linked device
callee is checked in each root's actual invocation tree. A native comparison
executes the corresponding materialised region/callee closure under its
recorded layout, input and tool configuration (dialect §§17.7, 19.5, 19.9).

These notes specify boundary scope and exact remaining contract work; they
implement no container, host shim or memory registration. The existing
bennett-4ht issue tracks that publication/implementation obligation. Current
device contracts and their complete estimate/proof requirements remain in
force; an unimplemented boundary is not evidence that mixed execution works.
### I07 — Full lifted LLVM closure

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.7`. **Group:** 0.2.11. **Decision:** D04. **Audit:** D4, B-39.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 BD-E-LIFT-SUBSET excluded memory, calls, FP and vectors, including ordinary SQLite query paths.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-16.1-P1 deletes the diagnostic and admits every materialised function; DLT-12.2-P38/C enumerate 546 generic intrinsics, and DLT-16.4 maps memory/calls/aggregates/carriers.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** ISA-4.2, ISA-3.7 and ISA-7.2 provide memory, allocation, lproc recursion and lifted control; E17.1 has no location-width cap, but the existing canonical dialect limb map remains valid.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> Every materialised function in an oracle closure MUST remain liftable through the complete normalisation and mapping tables, including memory, recursion, indirect calls, vectors and floating-point carriers. [deps: DLT-12.2, DLT-C, DLT-16.1-P1, DLT-16.4, ISA-3.7, ISA-4.2, ISA-7.2]

**Rejected alternatives:** A scalar-only profile, silently trapping an unsupported generic intrinsic, or limiting the contract to checker seeds narrows scope.

**Conformance consequence:** DN/DM/DV/DR/DF/DX: retain all N0–N35 and V1–V16 rows; memory/recursive/wide/vector/FP/varargs witnesses, native comparison and prefix reversal; SQLite full closure is the rung-4 gate, not a mini substitute.

**Dependency order:** I33, I17, I22/I35, I15, I37, I12, I36; map after normalisation.

**Maintainer choice:** D04 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

### I08 — Requested deferred measurement
### I15 — All seven FP formats and vector/libm FP

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.15`. **Group:** 0.2.11. **Decision:** D04. **Audit:** D16, B-45.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 N17 trapped half, bfloat, x86_fp80, fp128, transcendentals and vector FP beyond f32/f64.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-13.1 through DLT-13.5 already cover seven formats including ppc_fp128, own-format carrier routines, bit-exact retained forms and constrained environments; DLT-12.1-P1 puts N33 before N32.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** ISA-4.1 and ISA-4.2 integer operations, memory and calls implement soft float; no new FP ISA is required. Libraries remain separately implemented contract consumers.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> All seven formats MUST retain own-format arithmetic and transcendental semantics, with vector operations scalarised first and identical deterministic carrier implementations in the materialised native and lifted builds. [deps: DLT-13.1, DLT-13.2, DLT-13.3, DLT-13.4, DLT-13.5, DLT-12.1-P1, ISA-4.2]

**Rejected alternatives:** Silent widening through f64/native long double, deleting retained fneg/transport, or faulting uncovered checker formats would narrow the accepted contract.

**Conformance consequence:** DF/DN/DM: seven-format bit/NaN/rounding/carrier and pair-result probes, vector reductions, powi exponent width, fmt_fp and DX8 libm. Keep V10 carrier positives and arithmetic residue negatives.

**Dependency order:** I07 map foundation, N21 before FP rewrite and repeated N22 after wide conversions.

**Maintainer choice:** D04 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

### I16 — Mixed LLVM majors
### I38 — Whole-program reversible mode

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.38`. **Group:** 0.3. **Decision:** D18. **Audit:** O-04.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 had no marker-free entry route for unmodified programs.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-19.2 already synthesises a CC launch stub and R entry, argv/envp, exit status, device tape and reversal, but DLT-11.5-P2 applies BLK-5 to every R entry.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** ISA-10, ISA-7.3 and ISA-7.4 and K5/K6 cover whole-run completed/failed reversal with explicit eligibility; faulting R calls do not return a recoverable status to CC.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> Whole-program R mode MUST retain the unmodified entry route and effect tape, and MUST report terminal faults and reverse eligible completed or failed runs according to ISA 0.9. [deps: DLT-19.2, DLT-14.7, DLT-18.6, ISA-4.9-P1, ISA-7.4, ISA-10]

**Rejected alternatives:** Adding source markers to musl/kernel main narrows unmodified execution; executing a CC successor after R failure contradicts K5.

**Conformance consequence:** DD/DE/DS: argv/env/status, puts tape forward/reverse/replay, failed run and open activation reversal; DX3/5/6 actual programs remain gates beyond the minis.

**Dependency order:** I-bz6, I13/I36/I37, I32 when program allocates registers; D01 implementation gate.

**Maintainer choice:** D18 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

<a id="i40"></a>
### I40 — Runtime entry-state snapshots

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.40`. **Group:** 0.3. **Decision:** D19. **Audit:** O-07.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 omitted transfer of caller-mutated globals/heap and allocator metadata into an oracle, including a post-boot snapshot.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-8.5 and DLT-16.2-P20 already specify replay with sizes/liveness/bump, including allocation-only closures; DLT-8.5-P7 still needs BLK-4 when R prepares the snapshot.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** ISA-7.1 and ISA-7.2 plus qreg concat and calls already realise replay/copy; K2 now permits preparation/release in R. G-12 overlay is absent but not needed for capability.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> Runtime oracle entry state MUST retain caller bytes and allocator replay in native address order before any wrapper-private allocation, and R callers MUST use reversible register preparation and release for that same snapshot construction. [deps: DLT-8.5, DLT-16.2-P20, ISA-7.1, ISA-7.2, ISA-3.3-P18, ISA-7.4]

**Rejected alternatives:** Link-time images alone omit runtime state; resetting an allocation-only closure's allocator changes addresses; zeroing replayed inputs or silently requiring a small snapshot narrows the kernel path.

**Conformance consequence:** DO/DM/DD/DS: entry-replay-byte, entry-allocation-only and driver-snapshot-initializer; add R snapshot + reverse through metadata changes and stale cache invalidation on alloc/free/stack restore. DX7 compares the same runtime snapshot per basis input.

**Dependency order:** I33/I17/I32/I-bz6; fixed build snapshot remains a compatible path, G-12 is efficiency only.

**Maintainer choice:** D19 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

## 9. Objects, identity, linking, reversible ABI (object 0.9)

### 9.1 Sections and schemas

Order fixed: `symbols imports functions data devices layout schedule
certificate coverage`; each at most once; unknown sections or fields are
errors. Metadata sections are one JCS line each, an object:

```
  symbols:  { "<local index>": { "name", "kind": "cc"|"r"|"oracle"|"action"|"perm_action"|"lproc"|"data", "signature": <sig>, "interface_hash" } }
  imports:  { "<name>": { "signature": <sig>, "pinned_closure_hash"?: hex } }
  devices:  { "models": { "<dev>": { "version": "0.9" } }, "services": { "<reverse-dns id>": { "version": "…" } } }
  layout:   { "ptr_width", "H", "A_tab", "S_cells", "null_size", "heap_base", "heap_limit_max",
              "globals": { "<name>": { "base", "size", "image": "<data name>" } },
              "interface_regions": { "<oracle>": { "<region>": { "base" } } }, "block_image"?: "<data name>" }
  schedule: §11      certificate: §12      coverage: { "entries": [ §13 ] }
  <sig>:    kind "oracle": { "tier":"q", "kind", "in":[w…], "tgt":w, "fault":F, "mem":[{"A","W","role"}], "args":[types], "abi":"0.9" }
            kind "action"/"perm_action": { "tier":"q", "kind", "mut":[w…], "in":[w…], "mem":[…], "args":[types], "bounds":[…], "abi" }
            kind "lproc": { "tier":"q", "kind", "params":[w…], "result":w, "tokens":["token"…], "args":[types], "mem":[{"A","W","role":"in"}], "abi" }
            kind "cc"/"r": { "tier", "kind", "params":[types], "results":[types], "abi" }
            kind "data": { "tier":"-", "kind", "size", "abi" }
```

Integers that may exceed 2^53 are decimal strings, and a JSON number ≥
2^53 anywhere in metadata is an error. `data` lines are `NAME SP HEX`.
**Encodings.** `types` in `args`, `params` and `results` are the §16.2
type spellings as JSON strings (`"i64"`, `"ptr"`, `"f64"`, `"token"`,
`"qreg[8]"`); `w` in `in`, `tgt`, `mut`, lproc `params` and `result` is
the integer bundle or location width (§16.3). An lproc's `tokens`,
`args` and `mem` arrays are mandatory (empty arrays when absent from the
header); `args` excludes tokens and `mem` has only `in` roles (§3.7).
Array order is header order within each group. A **program-point id**
(§9.2) is the JSON array `[interface_hash, block name, ordinal]`.
Certificate `frame` fields use exactly the typed-record schema of §9.4;
unknown fields are errors there as in every metadata record.
`devices.models` lists every §7.3 model that an `effect` of the object
uses (an `effect` on an unlisted §7.3 model is a load error; a device
outside §7.3 is never listed and faults at run time, §17 item 3; a key
that is not a §7.3 model is an unknown field); `devices.services` lists
every `hostcall` service (§10). An interface region's name (its param
name and its `layout.interface_regions.<oracle>` key) MUST differ from
every symbol and import name of the object, since `@name` in `lift.addr`
denotes either (§9.2).

### 9.2 Identity

An lproc's token, classical and region parameter groups are part of
its JCS signature (§9.1), including their order and types. The header
and every forwarded operand list are canonical function bytes; changing
them changes the envelope and, through references, the caller's closure
hash. Fixed binding instances are derived per oracle (§3.7), not new
symbols or separately hashed bodies; instance rows share the source
row's program-point id and expand once per fixed row instance per step,
while the instance and semantic-call occurrence components in §11
invocation paths and §12.1 context keys distinguish their uses. [deps: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-12.1-P11] Entry argument values and token tuples bind invocations through
`params_hash`, not through the lproc's interface hash (this section).
Imports must match all five groups; validation and instance formation
are repeated after linking (§9.3).

**Canonical function bytes**: the text from the function's header line
through its last instruction line, lines joined by LF, no trailing LF.
**Semantic envelope** of a symbol (byte-exact; every part ends with LF):

```
  (1) "isa=" <full version string, e.g. 0.9>
  (2) "ptr_width=" INT
  (3) the JCS signature
  (4) the canonical function bytes (with reference tokens replaced, below); for a data symbol: its data line NAME SP HEX
  (5a) for every data entry in the closure set, sorted by name bytewise:        "data " NAME " " HEX
  (5b) for every layout leaf in the closure set, sorted by JCS path bytes: "layout " <JCS key-component array> " " <JCS value>. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1]
  (5c) for an R/CC function, for every device model and service it uses, sorted: "device " <name> " " <JCS entry>
  (6) for every referenced symbol outside the symbol's SCC, in order of first reference: its 64-hex hash
```

**Closure set**, by symbol kind. **Oracle**: **all** `layout` leaves of
the object except `interface_regions` entries of other oracles (every
global's pre-entry affects its allocation faults), its own `interface_
regions.<oracle>.*` leaves, and every data image those leaves name.
**Lproc**: the leaves and images of every global `@g` it names in `lift.
addr`. **Action / permutation action**: no layout leaves; the `@image`
of each `qregion` is an ordinary symbol reference (replaced in part (4),
listed in part (6)). **CC / R function**: all `layout` leaves, every
data image they name, and in (5c) every device model and `hostcall`
service it uses. **Data symbol**: parts (5)–(6) are empty. A layout leaf
is a scalar or an object at the deepest level the schema lists (`globals.
<name>` and `interface_regions.<oracle>.<region>` are leaves; `ptr_
width`, `H`, … are leaves); a scalar's JCS value is its decimal text.
**Globals are data symbols**: every global `g` in `layout.globals` MUST
have `image` = `g` and the object MUST define a `data` symbol named `g`
of that size, so the token `@g` in `lift.addr` is both the global and a
symbol reference (replaced by the data symbol's hash; the global's
`base` and `size` enter through the layout leaf). An `@name` naming an
interface region (in `lift.addr`) is not a symbol reference and is left
unchanged. **Reference replacement**: in parts
(4)–(5) every `@name` symbol-reference token is replaced by `@` followed
by the referenced symbol's 64-hex hash (the symbol's own name in its
header line is a definition, not a reference, and is left unchanged); the **interface hash** uses
interface hashes of defined symbols and `SHA-256(name ‖ 0x00 ‖ JCS
signature)` (hex) for unresolved imports; the **closure hash** uses
closure hashes. **Acyclic and cyclic symbols.** The reference graph
has an edge from a symbol to every symbol it references (parts
(4)–(5)). An **acyclic** symbol — a one-member SCC that does not
reference itself — has hash = SHA-256 of its own envelope (example 1's
`inc8`, example 2's `zero` and `sum_db`); the SCC procedure below applies
to every **cyclic** SCC, including a one-member SCC whose function
references itself (example 2's `sum`). **SCCs**: members ordered by name bytewise; every intra-
SCC `@name` token (parts (4)–(5)) is replaced by `@$<decimal index>`;
intra-SCC references contribute no part-(6) line; `scc_hash` = SHA-256
over the concatenation of the members' envelopes in that order; member
hash = SHA-256 over the ASCII bytes of `<scc_hash hex>:<decimal index>`.
**object_hash** = SHA-256 of the canonical object text with the
`schedule`, `certificate` and `coverage` sections deleted together with
the blank line preceding each. `reference_program_hash` = the oracle's
interface hash. `params_hash` = SHA-256 of the JCS array of classical
parameter values (the typed encoding of §12.3, K13); `schedule_hash` =
SHA-256 of the schedule's JCS bytes.
**Program-point id** = `(symbol interface hash, block name, instruction
ordinal within the block counting nested lines in text order)`.
Executable certificates and caches bind to **closure hashes** (required,
§12).

A layout leaf's path is the JCS array of its original JSON key
components from the layout root to the deepest schema-listed leaf,
so a scalar has a one-component path and an interface-region leaf has
path `["interface_regions","<oracle>","<region>"]`.
[deps: ISA-9.1-P1, ISA-16.1]
Part (5b) emits exactly one line for each structured leaf in the closure
set, including distinct leaves whose dotted renderings coincide, ordered
by the unsigned UTF-8 bytes of that JCS path array, independently of the
leaf values; no dotted rendering is used as a key or an ordering key.
[deps: ISA-9.1-P1, ISA-16.1]
An oracle's own interface-region leaves and entries of other oracles
are selected by equality of the original JSON key under
`interface_regions` to the oracle's name, never by a prefix of a
rendered path; an lproc's global leaves are selected by equality of
the key under `globals` to the global name.
[deps: ISA-3.7-P1, ISA-9.1-P1]

**Envelope boundary lemma** (non-normative).
For a function symbol, §16.2 requires a header beginning `fn `,
`oracle `, `action `, `perm_action ` or `lproc `; following lines are
`regs`, block labels, instructions, or structured closing braces. [deps: ISA-16.2]
No §4 opcode is `data`, `layout` or `device`; a result with one of
those names has ` = ` after the name, which cannot begin any part-(5)
line (a data name, a JSON array, or a device name is required there). [deps: ISA-4.8, ISA-9.1, ISA-16.2]
No canonical function line is a bare 64-hex string, so the first
part-(5)/(6) line ends the function bytes unambiguously. [deps: ISA-4.8, ISA-16.2]
The fixed `data `, `layout `, `device ` prefixes distinguish (5a–c),
and a bare 64-hex line distinguishes (6); JSON strings escape LF. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1]
For a data symbol, the signature kind selects exactly one data line
for (4), with (5)–(6) empty, even when the data name itself is `data`,
`layout` or `device`. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.2]
Together with the fixed first three lines and the injective JCS array
paths, these facts make the envelope parts recoverable without length
prefixes; this is an encoding argument, not a claim of SHA-256 injectivity. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1, ISA-16.2, ISA-9.2-P4]

### 9.3 Linking

Imports resolve by name + signature (mismatch: link error; pinned hash:
equality required); resolved closure hashes are recorded in the linked
certificate. Equal-closure-hash duplicates deduplicate; unequal under one
name, or conflicting `devices`/`layout`/`data` entries: link error.
Linking = resolution, validation, certificate composition (§12.3),
rebinding to closure hashes.

### 9.4 Reversible ABI


 succeeded in 0ms:
### 17.7 The native comparison build

Differential testing (NS §5.1) compares the artefact with the **native comparison binary**: the
materialised device variants, code-generated by the same LLVM major and linked with
`bennett-runtimes` and `bennett-arith` (never host libc or libm) under the comparison link of
§19.5. It is never an `-O0` build: optimisation legally changes marker operands (spike F4), and
the materialised optimised module is the one program both builds implement. The driver MUST make
division by zero and INT_MIN/−1 trap in the native build: on targets whose divide instruction does
not trap it inserts, after materialisation and outside the materialised module, a check that
calls `__bennett_nz_fault` with code 5 or 6. Results compare by the outcome table of §18.7.

**Comparison-only support exception.** Only after the materialised device module has been fixed, the native comparison backend MAY define the three `__bennett_nz_` primitives of §3.1 as native support, and the driver MUST emit `__bennett_rt_native_layout` as specified in §19.5; these definitions MUST NOT enter producer inputs, production device bitcode, region identity or the materialised module used by the ISA map.

The native primitive support MUST reproduce the resolved device addresses, zero fill, live allocation sizes, nonreclaiming allocation/free history and fault outcomes of §§17.1/17.5, with capacity for every history admitted by the resolved heap budget (at most one heap record per byte since max(n,1) ≥ 1), without an arbitrary smaller record cap; host setup/resource failure MUST terminate comparison with an explicit infrastructure failure, never silently relocate the heap, choose defaults, use host malloc, or report a successful dialect outcome.

**Definedness classifiers.** The comparison binary MUST detect the materialised memory faults of §17.5 (out-of-live-allocation access, use after free and invalid/double free), division by zero and signed division overflow, and reached explicit fault/unreachable paths; instrumentation or a checked memory adapter may supply these checks outside the materialised module, and UBSan-only coverage MUST NOT be reported as satisfying the memory-fault obligation.

The harness MUST separately classify source inputs using instrumented original-input builds or a checked reference interpreter as in NS §3.2, report flagged inputs as undefined/excluded, and record the classifier's actual detection coverage; no classifier claims source UB erased by optimisation is detectable in the comparison binary.

## 18. Reference semantics
### 19.9 Options, identity, outputs, module flag

| Option | Meaning | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `-bennett-dialect=<v>` | MUST equal the plugin's dialect version (`BD-E-VERSION`) | required |
| `-bennett-isa=<v>` | MUST equal the ISA version the plugin targets (`BD-E-VERSION`) | `0.8` |
| `--bennett-budgets=<file>`, `--bennett-budget-default=<s,h,st,d,ss>` | §9.2 | §9.2 defaults |
| `--bennett-class=<name>` | §3.4 | none |
| `--bennett-tier=reversible`, `--bennett-entry=<sym>`, `--bennett-disk=<file>` | §19.2 | – |
| `--bennett-oracle=<sym>`, `--bennett-bind=…`, `--bennett-dev=…`, `--bennett-payload=…` | §19.3 | – |
| `--bennett-snapshot=<dump>` | §19.6 | – |
| `-bennett-closed-unit` | §19.7 | off |
| `--bennett-platform=<profile>` | §20 | none |
| `--bennett-vscale=<V>`, `--bennett-cpu=<model>` | §12.5, §15.2 | §24 |
| `--bennett-qreg-instances=<n>` | §6.5 | 64 |
| `-bennett-inplace-exhaustive-bits=<n>` | §8.7 | 24 |
| `-bennett-warn-qstore` | §6.7 | off |
| `-bennett-out=<dir>` | the outputs below | required |

**Identity.** `llvmGetPassPluginInfo` returns plugin name `bennett` and version string
`dialect-0.2.12;isa-0.8`; the driver refuses a plugin whose string differs. [deps: DLT-19.9, DLT-23] One plugin build per LLVM
major (NS §3.2).

**Outputs** (in `-bennett-out`, file names plugin-defined, §24 D22): the materialised device
variants as bitcode (§17.7); the host module with device entries replaced by launch stubs; the ISA
objects (ISA §9, §16); the certificate (ISA §12) with `producer.materialisation = {llvm_version,
pipeline, normaliser_version: "0.2.8"}` and `bindings.dialect_mapping_version = "0.2.9"`; the coverage
report (ISA §13), whose `source` field is `dialect-0.2.12:<rule id>` (e.g. `dialect-0.2.12:N33`); the
blocker reports (§25.1); and the **build record**, one JCS object with the build flags, the
budget table (resolved, including each shape's selection_identity, final semantic identity,
resolved components and frozen layout capacities under §9.2) and its SHA-256, the runtimes, arith and asm-table hashes, the snapshot
hash, the layout options and the `cpuid` leaf table (§15.2). Its SHA-256 is recorded as the build metadata of the certificate's
`producer.version` (`<driver version>+build.<hex>`) until the ISA certificate has a field for it
(§25.2). [deps: DLT-19.9, DLT-25.2]

**A missing plugin fails loud.** No production artefact defines a marker: `bennett-runtimes`,
B1–B17, questions E1–E14).

---

## 1. Scope and conformance

### 1.1 What the dialect is

The dialect is the contract between **front ends** that emit LLVM IR containing calls to
`__bennett_*` marker functions and the **plugin** that, at the final link of a whole program,
recognises those calls, analyses and normalises the code they reach, and maps it onto the ISA
(ISA §1.1). It fixes:

- the marker ABI, operand encodings and C header (§3);
- the processing order, execution tiers, polyvariant variants and function addresses (§4);
- the device analysis: points-to, value sets and every question answered from them (§5);
- the handle convention, provenance, comparison and collections of handles (§6);
- regions: kinds, candidate sets, dispatch, identity, well-formedness (§7);
- the oracle interface: the descriptor, parameter and device bindings, entry state, wide outputs
  and in-place updates (§8);
- budgets (§9);
- taint-driven lifting of classical code under control (§10);
- effects and device models per tier (§11);
- the materialised normalisation and the residual-poison validator (§12), floating point
  (§13), calls and control transfer (§14), inline asm and target intrinsics (§15);
- the lifting map from materialised LLVM to ISA objects (§16);
- the device classical memory model, CC and R lowering rules and fault outcomes (§17);
- a reference semantics from marker programs to the ISA formal model (§18);
- the driver's modes and plugin-invocation interface (§19);
- the platform profile `riscv64-bennett-virt` (§20);
- the legality corpus and conformance suites (§21); diagnostics (§22); versioning (§23);
  deferred mechanics (§24); blockers and ISA items (§25).

It does not fix the plugin's optimisation pipeline after normalisation, the plugin's device
objects beyond the constraints of §18.7, or any format the ISA owns.

### 1.2 Conformance classes

| Class | Who | Obligation |
|---|---|---|
| Producer | front ends (clang, rustc, Bennett.jl, importers) | emit modules that satisfy §3 and the outlining conventions of §7.6; never emit a reserved name of §3.1 other than a marker |
| Plugin | `bennett-plugin` and competitors | the processing order of §4.1; give every module the verdict the rules give it, with the diagnostics of §22; produce ISA objects whose execution equals the reference semantics (§18.7); suites DA–DX (§21.3) |
| Driver | `bennett-driver` | §19; links `bennett-runtimes` and `bennett-arith` bitcode of the link's LLVM major (§19.1); builds the comparison link and the native comparison binary (§17.7); emits blocker reports (§25) |
| Validator | the plugin's validator and `bennett-conformance` | the residual-poison validator (§12.4) and the reference recogniser (§3, §5, §6, §7), implemented independently; suites DA–DN |
| Runtime | `bennett-runtimes` and `bennett-arith` | define every entry point that §11, §13, §14, §17 and §20 name (prefixes `__bennett_rt_`, `__bennett_dev_`, `__bennett_sf_`, §3.1) with the semantics stated there, as ordinary bitcode compiled by the same pipeline; suites DR and DF |
| Platform | a platform profile of §20 | a board model plus shims satisfying §20; suite DP |

`scripts/dialect_check.py` in this repository is the seed of the pre-flight checker (§1.5). It
never bounds this document: an example it cannot yet execute fails the check (rule P-2, §1.5).

### 1.3 Relation to the ISA specification

The ISA is settled; this document never redefines it. Every ISA notion used here (tiers, wires,
`qreg` handles, oracles, actions, permutation actions, lprocs, F_B, budgets, tokens, fault
codes, the reference interpreter, interface and closure hashes, layouts, the launch ABI) has
exactly the meaning ISA §2–§17 give it. Where the dialect needs a construct ISA 0.8 lacks, this
document states (a) the full dialect mechanism, (b) its realisation on ISA 0.8 where one exists,
which may be a longer construction (an **efficiency item**, §25.3), and (c) otherwise a
**blocker** (§25.1) naming the exact ISA change. The dialect never shrinks to fit the ISA and
never extends the ISA unilaterally.

sequence still has its ordinal-0 carrier at its release point. [deps: ISA-4.5, ISA-6.2-P1, ISA-6.4-P1, ISA-11-P6]

**Source cleanup-pair partition (K81).** Consumers MUST form the following
partition from the source before K65 selection and K80 expansion. Match
source operation occurrences by §5.3 and §6.2, against the most recent
still-current computing occurrence on all of the operation's outputs;
matching restores its captured entry versions. A joint multi-output
operation and its inverse are indivisible. This is occurrence/version
matching, not equality of the composed unitary alone.

An intrinsic `quncopy`, `qunload` or `lift.oracle_uncall` pairs only with
its matching `qcopy`, `qload` or `lift.oracle_call`, respectively. Its
computing sequence does not absorb subsequent changes to the produced
targets, and its cleanup consists only of that intrinsic instruction.
For `qfree` or `qreturn`, instead coalesce the maximal terminal chain of
matched source inverses restoring the released lifetime to its allocation
or borrow entry, together with that release. Terminal means that each
restored version is retained until the next inverse in the chain or the
release; an intervening new computation on that lifetime breaks the
chain. Rows acting only on disjoint values do not break it and are not
included. Intrinsic cleanup instructions remain boundaries and are never
absorbed. A joint inverse restoring several released lifetimes belongs
to the first such release in source execution order; later releases do
not own it again. Each remaining matched adjoint-call or oracle-call
inverse has its own K65 carrier. A primitive inverse outside a
release-ended chain has no separate K65 carrier. Thus every explicit
cleanup inverse occurrence has exactly one owner; pair formation neither
requires textual adjacency nor depends on the selected realisation.
Use instantiated source order, including §4.6 controls and iterations,
without entering semantic callees or K64 support subtrees. K65's private
constructor cleanups remain excluded, and §6's validity conditions still
apply; this partition adds no source-program restriction.

For an independent call pair, the pre-call mutable targets need not be
zero. Its cleanup restores their exact pre-call versions. Recompute
therefore includes the exact source-derived pre-call input history in
its fresh computation, uncomputes that fresh history, and restores the
pre-call targets rather than releasing or zeroing them. For a permutation
on wire targets with pre-call value h and post-call value f(h), a
zero-based fresh computation of f(h) XOR h, XORed into the current
targets and then uncomputed, gives this restoration. The history is
replayed input to that pair, not ownership of the earlier source rows;
K80 assigns the fresh replay's caller leaves and calls to this pair's
carrier. Other target kinds retain their instruction-defined inverse
and §6.4's legality conditions.

**Intrinsic-boundary witness (K81).** The consumer's reduced source is:

```
entry:0  d = qcopy x
entry:1  action_call @mix (d[0], d[1]) () () ()
entry:2  adj action_call @mix (d[0], d[1]) () () ()
entry:3  quncopy x d
mix:     cx a b
```

Here x is `wire[2]`, and mix is the permutation CX on two mutable bits.
The displayed two-slot indexed list is schematic: §16.2's canonical
NAME-only list spells the same operation as `action_call @mix (d) () () ()`
with `mix (a:wire[2] mut)` and body `cx a[0] a[1]`.
The pairs are computation `{entry:1}` / cleanup `{entry:2}`, carrier
`entry:2`, and computation `{entry:0}` / cleanup `{entry:3}`, carrier
`entry:3`. There are no other carriers. Neither carrier owns a row of
the other pair. Both use cleanup ordinal 0 and can independently select
reverse or recompute; duplicate maximal cleanup entries at `entry:2`
tie there even when `entry:3` has a unique entry. Reverse retains the
call at `entry:2/call_index:0` and the intrinsic inverse-copy/release
leaves at `entry:3`. For call recompute, h=x: compute `mix(x) XOR x`
into fresh u, XOR u into d, then uncompute/release u. The fresh mix and
adjoint-mix have call_index 0 and 1 at `entry:2`; their callee CXs each
have ordinal 0. The original call at `entry:1/call_index:0` is unchanged.
The later quncopy still restores/releases d independently at `entry:3`.
Inserting disjoint rows leaves those two pairs intact. Replacing the
call pair by two primitive CX rows leaves only the quncopy carrier;
replacing qcopy/quncopy by qalloc/qfree instead gives one release-ended
carrier owning the inverse row and release, as in K80's witness below.
it binds an initially inactive allocation declaration for its lexical
body, including captured actual bodies. It may not shadow any binding.
An `allocation` formal (exactly `name`,`type`) passes that declaration
by identity, including metadata and live/inactive status. Exactly
`{"acquire": name}` emits its unconditional allocation and changes it
from inactive to live; exactly `{"relinquish": name}` emits its matching
unconditional release and changes it from live to inactive. An acquire
requires inactive, a relinquish requires live, and the declaration must
be inactive when its lexical body exits. While live, its name is a wire
actual of its declared width, including for subsequent caller constructors
after `invoke` returns; while inactive any wire use rejects. Captures and
invocations share the same status, not a copy of the lifetime. Inverse
exchanges acquire/relinquish, using the same declaration metadata and
occurrence identity. Reacquisition denotes a new allocation occurrence,
with fresh identity even within one declaration. Clean release requires
zero; borrowed release requires restoration on the full domain (§5.6),
never zero. These are user/instruction allocation results; internal
library scratch still obeys the clean LIFO rule below. A declaration
or allocation formal listed in `scratch` when acquired marks that entire
allocation lifetime as library scratch; the mark follows its identity
through captures and invokes, requires clean state and cannot be removed
by releasing through a different formal name. Allocations are
unconditional even under a false inherited control.
Witness: declare out[2] of class ancilla; invoke an allocation recipe on
out; apply two X gates to out[1] in separate caller/callee bodies; invoke
its release recipe. All leaves refer to the one live allocation identity
and release it clean. Wire use before acquisition/after release, double
acquisition, unmatched release or leaving the declaration live rejects.
A borrowed declaration preserves the exact class and both initial values
of every borrowed bit; no conversion to class any is permitted.

**Angles and quantum witnesses (K20).** An `angle` actual is a bound angle
formal or a finite binary64 value. Reusing §12.3's bit-pattern encoding:
`f64` is `0x` followed by exactly 16 lowercase hex digits of its IEEE 754
binary64 bit pattern, most significant nibble first (so −0 and NaN payloads
are not lost). Angles additionally reject NaN and infinities (§3.1).
Only `rx`, `ry`, `rz` have `params`, an array containing exactly one angle;
other §4.4 gates have no angle parameters. No recipe arithmetic on angles
is permitted. `inverse` negates each rotation angle by toggling bit 63,
including signed zero, and exchanges s/sdg and t/tdg as §5.3 requires.

ill-formed. [deps: ISA-4.3-P2, ISA-12.1-P8.S4]
A `borrowed` entry MUST NOT discharge an interface bundle or region-cell
coverage obligation, whatever its name. [deps: ISA-4.3-P2, ISA-5.6-P1, ISA-12.1-P8]

**Domain validity and omission (K71/K72).** After resolution, each
component MUST occur at most once, every supplied `width` MUST equal
that component's width, ranges MUST satisfy 0 ≤ lo ≤ hi < 2^width, and
cell indices MUST satisfy 0 ≤ index < 2^A; a violation rejects the
claim as ill-formed. [deps: ISA-3.3-P2.S2, ISA-3.3-P19, ISA-12.2-P1]
Every omitted component is held at zero on every replayed element,
including omitted borrowed positions after the listed entries; workspace
and local regions retain their separate zero/image initialisations.
[deps: ISA-5.2-P4, ISA-5.3-P1, ISA-12.2-P1]
The domain denotes the product of the resolved ranges with those
omitted components fixed at zero, not the full range of an omitted
component. [deps: ISA-5.2-P4, ISA-5.3-P1, ISA-12.2-P1]
For an exhaustive claim its element count n is the product of the range
sizes over individual resolved components, the empty product being 1,
and its covered fraction is exactly n/|D|, with |D| the product of the
full component range sizes (prepended inherited controls included).
[deps: ISA-5.2-P4, ISA-5.3-P1, ISA-12.1-P8, ISA-12.2-P1]

**Coverage and verdicts (K69/K74/K85).**

A domain MAY contain fixed_regions:[{name,A,W,image,sha256}]. An absent
field is an empty list. name resolves by the existing component rules to
an in-role interface region, with precisely the subject's A and W.
Each resolved fixed region occurs exactly once and MUST NOT also occur
in domain.regions. A mut-role region cannot be fixed by this mechanism.

image is exactly ceil(2^A*W/8) raw bytes encoded as lowercase hexadecimal;
unused high bits in the final byte MUST be zero. Cell i consists of bits
[iW,(i+1)W) in the little-endian byte image. sha256 is SHA-256 of the raw
bytes, not the hexadecimal text. The checker hashes independently and
uses those cells on every replay. Fixed cells participate in domain
validity and tuple/output checks, but each contributes one choice to n.
The full unrestricted |D| remains unchanged. This metadata introduces no
operation for inspecting unknown coherent state.

After every §12.1 replay obligation succeeds, an exhaustive
table/program/oracle claim receives exactly one outcome, in this order:
(1) proof iff its domain equals unrestricted D component by component;
(2) otherwise proof_relative_to_image iff fixed_regions is nonempty,
    every image binding is valid, every fixed region is accounted for,
    and every remaining component occurs exactly once with its full
    range (all interface bundles/cells, borrowed positions and prepended
    inherited controls included);
(3) otherwise evidence.
Successful sampled claims always receive evidence, including when their
distinct draws cover the whole domain. Malformed domains, stale hashes
and mismatches reject, rather than receiving any successful verdict.
Omission-at-zero remains a strict slice unless the original component
is thereby genuinely at its full range under the existing rules.

**Date:** 2026-11-01. **Status:** draft 0.3, not yet graded; the orchestrator convenes the council at an acceptance

The **full version string** (`0.3`) is the compatibility unit (§23): drafts are not compatible with each other. [deps: DLT-front-P2, DLT-23]

**Standing rule (CLAUDE.md rule 10; council rubric S0).** This document narrows no capability
that the north star promises (its thesis, §3–§5 and every rung of the §8 demo ladder; Grover
over unmodified SQLite is the core). It rejects at compile time only what the north star itself
requires to be rejected or what a maintainer decision settles; every such rejection is a row of
§22.1 citing the clause or decision. A deferral (§24) postpones mechanics only and states the
capability it keeps. Where the ISA cannot express a promised capability in some case, the
dialect still specifies the whole mechanism, states the realisation that exists for the
other cases, and names a blocker (§25) whose verdict is `blocked:`, never `reject:`. On ISA 0.9
no blocker is active (§25.1).

D1–D25, B-01–B-55, O-01–O-08, G-12–G-16, ISA-1–ISA-11 are cited unchanged); the rung-4 spike
(`docs/spikes/sqlite/REPORT.md`, cited "SQLite §n"); the outline (cited "outline", decisions
B1–B17, questions E1–E14).

---

## 1. Scope and conformance

### 1.1 What the dialect is

The dialect is the contract between **front ends** that emit LLVM IR containing calls to
`__bennett_*` marker functions and the **plugin** that, at the final link of a whole program,
recognises those calls, analyses and normalises the code they reach, and maps it onto the ISA
(ISA §1.1). It fixes:

- the marker ABI, operand encodings and C header (§3);
- the processing order, execution tiers, polyvariant variants and function addresses (§4);
- the device analysis: points-to, value sets and every question answered from them (§5);
- the handle convention, provenance, comparison and collections of handles (§6);
- regions: kinds, candidate sets, dispatch, identity, well-formedness (§7);
- the oracle interface: the descriptor, parameter and device bindings, entry state, wide outputs
  and in-place updates (§8);
- budgets (§9);
- taint-driven lifting of classical code under control (§10);
- effects and device models per tier (§11);
- the materialised normalisation and the residual-poison validator (§12), floating point
  (§13), calls and control transfer (§14), inline asm and target intrinsics (§15);
- the lifting map from materialised LLVM to ISA objects (§16);
- the device classical memory model, CC and R lowering rules and fault outcomes (§17);
- a reference semantics from marker programs to the ISA formal model (§18);
- the driver's modes and plugin-invocation interface (§19);
- the platform profile `riscv64-bennett-virt` (§20);
- the legality corpus and conformance suites (§21); diagnostics (§22); versioning (§23);
  deferred mechanics (§24); blockers and ISA items (§25).

It does not fix the plugin's optimisation pipeline after normalisation, the plugin's device
objects beyond the constraints of §18.7, or any format the ISA owns.

### 1.2 Conformance classes

| Class | Who | Obligation |
|---|---|---|
| Producer | front ends (clang, rustc, Bennett.jl, importers) | emit modules that satisfy §3 and the outlining conventions of §7.6; never emit a reserved name of §3.1 other than a marker |
| Plugin | `bennett-plugin` and competitors | the processing order of §4.1; give every module the verdict the rules give it, with the diagnostics of §22; produce ISA objects whose execution equals the reference semantics (§18.7); suites DA–DX (§21.3) |
| Driver | `bennett-driver` | §19; links `bennett-runtimes` and `bennett-arith` bitcode of the link's LLVM major (§19.1); builds the comparison link and the native comparison binary (§17.7); emits blocker reports (§25) |
| Validator | the plugin's validator and `bennett-conformance` | the residual-poison validator (§12.4) and the reference recogniser (§3, §5, §6, §7), implemented independently; suites DA–DN |
| Runtime | `bennett-runtimes` and `bennett-arith` | define every entry point that §11, §13, §14, §17 and §20 name (prefixes `__bennett_rt_`, `__bennett_dev_`, `__bennett_sf_`, §3.1) with the semantics stated there, as ordinary bitcode compiled by the same pipeline; suites DR and DF |
| Platform | a platform profile of §20 | a board model plus shims satisfying §20; suite DP |

`scripts/dialect_check.py` in this repository is the seed of the pre-flight checker (§1.5). It
never bounds this document: an example it cannot yet execute fails the check (rule P-2, §1.5).

### 1.3 Relation to the ISA specification

The ISA is settled; this document never redefines it. Every ISA notion used here (tiers, wires,
`qreg` handles, oracles, actions, permutation actions, lprocs, F_B, budgets, tokens, fault
codes, the reference interpreter, interface and closure hashes, layouts, the launch ABI) has
exactly the meaning ISA §2–§17 give it. Where the dialect needs a construct ISA 0.9 lacks, this
document states (a) the full dialect mechanism, (b) its realisation on ISA 0.9 where one exists,
which may be a longer construction (an **efficiency item**, §25.3), and (c) otherwise a
**blocker** (§25.1) naming the exact ISA change. The dialect never shrinks to fit the ISA and
never extends the ISA unilaterally.

### 1.4 Departures from the north star

Only these remain. Every row of 0.1 §1.4 that narrowed scope (1.4-d, 1.4-e, 1.4-f) is deleted;
its replacement is §10, §9 and §8.7 respectively.

| North star | Dialect 0.2 | Why |
|---|---|---|
| §3.1 handle row: a handle through an `inttoptr`/`ptrtoint` round trip is rejected | Provenance flows through bitwise copies (§6.2) | Spike F1; maintainer decision 2; north-star amendment A2 (drafted with A1, bd `-25h`) |
| §3.1: regions entered as `__bennett_ctrl(q, @f, …)` with a constant `@f` | Region operands are classical data dispatched over a candidate set bounded by the program (§7.2) | Spike F3; maintainer decision 3 |
| Region named by symbol | Region identity is by content (§7.3) | Spike F2; maintainer decision 1 |
| §3.1 handle row is silent on comparison | `icmp eq/ne` of handles is identity (§6.3) | Maintainer decision 4 (2026-10-29; legality findings §3); a superset |
| §3.1, §4.2: a `qstore` into a register not known to be clean is a compile-time error | `qstore` is the ISA's XOR store; the compile-time rule is an optional warning `BD-W-QSTORE-UNCLEAN` (§6.7) | A register-granularity rule forbids the uncomputing second store; a superset of the north star (audit 1.4-g, D7) |
| §3.2: normalisation of "the outlined region" | Normalisation applies to every device variant (§12.1) | Device CC code is lowered to ISA CC, whose semantics differs from LLVM's poison semantics (ISA §4.1); a superset (audit 1.4-h) |

### 1.5 Notation and checked examples

LLVM IR is LLVM's textual form with opaque pointers, as accepted by LLVM 18, 20 and 23. `P(a)` is
the representation width of an integral pointer in address space a from DataLayout, falling
### 16.1 Liftable means materialised, always

Every materialised function is liftable (outline B1): §12 legalises every construct into integer
SSA values of at most 128 bits, memory, calls and branches at the map boundary.
Materialised LLVM may still contain bit-exact FP forms and aggregates; §16.4 maps these
through integer carriers and field locations. Every such accepted form has a row. 0.1's
`BD-E-LIFT-SUBSET` is deleted.

Every materialised function in an oracle closure MUST remain liftable through the complete normalisation and mapping tables, including memory, recursion, indirect calls, vectors and floating-point carriers. [deps: DLT-12.2, DLT-C, DLT-16.1-P1, DLT-16.4, ISA-3.7, ISA-4.2, ISA-7.2]

### 16.2 The reference object

For a shape with region function f, the reference object is the ISA text (ISA §16) with:

- the header `bennett-object isa=0.8 object=0.8 launch=0.8 sched=0.8 cert=0.8 coverage=0.8
  target=0.8 ptr_width=<pw>`;
- `section symbols`: one JCS line, keys `"0"`, `"1"`, … in the order `@ref`, `@L1` … `@Ln`, then
  the data symbols `G…` in `section data` order, each with its kind, name, signature (ISA §9.1;
  `args` types spelled as JCS strings of the ISA §16.2 type, e.g. `"i64"`) and interface hash
  (ISA §9.2);
- `section functions`: `@ref`, then the lprocs `@L1` … `@Ln`: one per function of the L closure of
  f (direct calls and indirect-call candidates), numbered in depth-first preorder from `@ref`
  along the `lift.call` rows in text order;
- `section data`: one line `G<n> <hex>` per global of the layout below, in `layout.globals` key
  order, with the global's initialiser bytes after §12 (function addresses encoded as A(F) in
  P(a)-bit little-endian at each pointer field, §4.4; pointers to globals as their bases plus offsets);
- `section layout`: one JCS line (§16.3).

**The wrapper `@ref`.** Its header is `oracle @ref (<ins>) (tgt:wire[T]) (fault:wire[4])
(<mems>) (<args>)`:
| N10 | `llvm.allow.sanitize.address`, `.hwaddress`, `.memory`, `.thread` | `false` (no sanitizer runtime exists in either build) |
| N10 | `llvm.get.dynamic.area.offset` | `0` |
| N10 | `llvm.vscale` | the constant V of §12.5 |
| N11 | calls of `llvm.trap`, `llvm.debugtrap`, `llvm.ubsantrap`, `llvm.looptrap` | F(4) |
| N11 | `llvm.cond.loop(c)` | the block is split after the call; `br i1 c, label %nz.spin.<f>.<k>, label <rest>`; the new block `nz.spin.<f>.<k>` is `br label %nz.spin.<f>.<k>` (the infinite loop LangRef defines; budgets bound it, §9) |
| N12 | `llvm.abs.*(x, i1 true)`, `llvm.ctlz.*(x, i1 true)`, `llvm.cttz.*(x, i1 true)` | the flag becomes `i1 false` |
| N13 | (0.1: `memcpy` → `memmove`) | replaced by N26 |
| N14 | `%r = shl/lshr/ashr iW a, b` with b not a constant | `%nz.c.r = icmp uge iW b, W`; `%nz.s.r = <op> iW a, b`; `%r = select i1 %nz.c.r, iW 0, iW %nz.s.r` (every shift by ≥ W gives 0, as the ISA's shifts do, ISA §4.1) |
| N14 | the same with b a constant ≥ W | every use of `%r` replaced by `0`; deleted |
| N15 | `extractelement`/`insertelement` with any integer index i on L lanes | interpret iK index i as unsigned, including negative spellings. A constant i<L is left for N21’s in-range lane renaming. A constant i>=L replaces all result uses by the zero element (extract) or the unchanged input vector (insert), then deletes the instruction. For a non-constant index: let W=max(K,⌈log2(L+1)⌉), zero-extend to `%nz.i.r` when W>K (otherwise use i unchanged); `%nz.c.r = icmp uge iW wide_i, L` with exact L; `%nz.e.r = <the original instruction>`; `%r = select i1 %nz.c.r, T Z, T %nz.e.r` with Z = the zero element (extract) or the unchanged vector operand (insert) |
| N16 | `%r = call T @llvm.fmuladd.*(a, b, c)` | `%nz.m.r = fmul T a, b`; `%r = fadd T %nz.m.r, c` (no fusion; explicit `llvm.fma` keeps fusion through §13) |
| N17 | (0.1: FP formats and transcendentals → F(8)) | replaced by §13 (N32, N33): no floating-point construct faults |
| N18 | (0.1: external calls in oracle and reversible closures → F(8)) | replaced by §4.5: shims first; trapped only without bitcode |
| N19 | floating-point arithmetic, comparison and conversion of `float` and `double` | the soft-float rewrite of §13.1 (applied by N32) |
| N20 | (0.1: alloca + memset) | replaced by N24 |
| N21 | **vector scalarisation**: every instruction of vector type, every vector intrinsic (`llvm.vector.*`, `llvm.experimental.vector.*`, `llvm.vp.*`, `llvm.experimental.vp.*`, `llvm.masked.*`, `llvm.stepvector`, `llvm.experimental.stepvector`, `llvm.get.active.lane.mask`, `llvm.experimental.get.vector.length`, `llvm.experimental.cttz.elts`, `llvm.vp.cttz.elts`, `llvm.loop.dependence.raw.mask`, `llvm.loop.dependence.war.mask`), vector parameters and results of internal device functions | per-lane scalar instructions in lane order, by the rules of §12.6 |
| N22 | **wide integers and non-byte memory**: an integer type iN with N > 128 in an instruction; a load or store of iN with N not a multiple of 8 or of an aggregate type | iN with N > 128 becomes L = ⌈N/64⌉ i64 limbs (limb 0 the least significant; the top limb is i(N − 64(L−1))) with the expansions of §12.7; a load or store of iN with N mod 8 ≠ 0 becomes a load or store of i(8⌈N/8⌉) with a `trunc` after the load and a `zext` before the store (fixing LangRef's unspecified extra bits as zero); a load or store of an aggregate becomes one access per element in element order at its offset (`%nz.e<k>.<r>`) |
| N23 | **varargs** | §14.3 |
| N24 | **allocas → shadow stack**: every `alloca`; `llvm.stacksave`, `llvm.stackrestore`; `llvm.frameaddress`, `llvm.sponentry`, `llvm.addressofreturnaddress`, `llvm.stackaddress`, `llvm.returnaddress`, `llvm.swift.async.context.addr`, `llvm.localescape`, `llvm.localrecover`, `llvm.localaddress`, `llvm.eh.recoverfp`, `llvm.eh.dwarf.cfa`; `llvm.structured.alloca` | §17.3 |
| N25 | `byval`, `inalloca` and `preallocated` arguments; `llvm.call.preallocated.*`; aggregate returns wider than 128 bits | an explicit caller copy into a shadow-stack slot of the caller's frame (`%nz.bv<j>.<r>` for argument j) passed as a plain `ptr`, and the attribute removed from the callee and the call; a return of a first-class aggregate wider than 128 bits is demoted to a hidden first parameter `ptr %nz.ret` in which the callee stores the value (`sret` semantics) |
| N26 | **memory intrinsics**: `llvm.memcpy.*`, `llvm.memcpy.inline.*`, `llvm.memmove.*`, `llvm.memset.*`, `llvm.memset.inline.*`, `llvm.memcpy/memmove/memset.element.unordered.atomic.*`, `llvm.experimental.memset.pattern.*` | `call void @__bennett_rt_memmove(ptr d, ptr s, i64 n)`, `call void @__bennett_rt_memset(ptr d, i8 v, i64 n)` (the `i1` volatile flag and element-size operand dropped; a constant length becomes an `i64` constant; a narrower non-constant length is first `%nz.n.<r> = zext … to i64`); `memset.pattern(d, v: T, count)` → a loop storing v at d + i·size(T) for i < count (blocks `nz.mp.<r>.*`) |
| N27 | **integer intrinsics** | the fixed expansions of §12.8 |
| N28 | **unwinding: explicit propagation** | §14.4 |
| N29 | **setjmp/longjmp** | §14.5 |
| N30 | **rewindable frames** (fibers) | §14.6, only in the functions §14.6 selects |
| N31 | **inline asm and target intrinsics** | the §15 tables |
| N32 | **floating point** | the soft-float rewrite of §13.1–§13.2 (after N21) |
| N33 | **extended FP formats and libm intrinsics** | §13.3–§13.5 |
| N34 | **R-tier budget instrumentation points** | a call of `__bennett_rt_rstep()` is *not* inserted; N34 marks each counted R instruction with metadata `!bennett.rstep` (kept by N3); lowering checks/charges before that instruction (§17.6). Batching must preserve the same first fault and effect prefix |
| N35 | **platform hooks** | §20.3 (MMIO routing), §20.4 (interrupt poll points), only under a platform profile |

 succeeded in 0ms:
to every **cyclic** SCC, including a one-member SCC whose function
references itself (example 2's `sum`). **SCCs**: members ordered by name bytewise; every intra-
SCC `@name` token (parts (4)–(5)) is replaced by `@$<decimal index>`;
intra-SCC references contribute no part-(6) line; `scc_hash` = SHA-256
over the concatenation of the members' envelopes in that order; member
hash = SHA-256 over the ASCII bytes of `<scc_hash hex>:<decimal index>`.
**object_hash** = SHA-256 of the canonical object text with the
`schedule`, `certificate` and `coverage` sections deleted together with
the blank line preceding each. `reference_program_hash` = the oracle's
interface hash. `params_hash` = SHA-256 of the JCS array of classical
parameter values (the typed encoding of §12.3, K13); `schedule_hash` =
SHA-256 of the schedule's JCS bytes.
**Program-point id** = `(symbol interface hash, block name, instruction
ordinal within the block counting nested lines in text order)`.
Executable certificates and caches bind to **closure hashes** (required,
§12).

A layout leaf's path is the JCS array of its original JSON key
components from the layout root to the deepest schema-listed leaf,
so a scalar has a one-component path and an interface-region leaf has
path `["interface_regions","<oracle>","<region>"]`.
[deps: ISA-9.1-P1, ISA-16.1]
Part (5b) emits exactly one line for each structured leaf in the closure
set, including distinct leaves whose dotted renderings coincide, ordered
by the unsigned UTF-8 bytes of that JCS path array, independently of the
leaf values; no dotted rendering is used as a key or an ordering key.
[deps: ISA-9.1-P1, ISA-16.1]
An oracle's own interface-region leaves and entries of other oracles
are selected by equality of the original JSON key under
`interface_regions` to the oracle's name, never by a prefix of a
rendered path; an lproc's global leaves are selected by equality of
the key under `globals` to the global name.
[deps: ISA-3.7-P1, ISA-9.1-P1]

**Envelope boundary lemma** (non-normative).
For a function symbol, §16.2 requires a header beginning `fn `,
`oracle `, `action `, `perm_action ` or `lproc `; following lines are
`regs`, block labels, instructions, or structured closing braces. [deps: ISA-16.2]
No §4 opcode is `data`, `layout` or `device`; a result with one of
those names has ` = ` after the name, which cannot begin any part-(5)
line (a data name, a JSON array, or a device name is required there). [deps: ISA-4.8, ISA-9.1, ISA-16.2]
No canonical function line is a bare 64-hex string, so the first
part-(5)/(6) line ends the function bytes unambiguously. [deps: ISA-4.8, ISA-16.2]
The fixed `data `, `layout `, `device ` prefixes distinguish (5a–c),
and a bare 64-hex line distinguishes (6); JSON strings escape LF. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1]
For a data symbol, the signature kind selects exactly one data line
for (4), with (5)–(6) empty, even when the data name itself is `data`,
`layout` or `device`. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.2]
Together with the fixed first three lines and the injective JCS array
paths, these facts make the envelope parts recoverable without length
prefixes; this is an encoding argument, not a claim of SHA-256 injectivity. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1, ISA-16.2, ISA-9.2-P4]

### 9.3 Linking

Imports resolve by name + signature (mismatch: link error; pinned hash:
equality required); resolved closure hashes are recorded in the linked
certificate. Equal-closure-hash duplicates deduplicate; unequal under one
name, or conflicting `devices`/`layout`/`data` entries: link error.
Linking = resolution, validation, certificate composition (§12.3),
rebinding to closure hashes.

### 9.4 Reversible ABI

- CC/R calls: classical frame; callee frames follow the call boundary;
  `uncall` reverses segments (§7.4).
- Region calls: interface bundles and regions passed in declared order,
  caller-owned; callee owns its workspace, returns it clean, local regions
  at image, borrowed bundles returned.
- **Frame descriptor** (certificate): interface slots retain their identities;
  scratch is recorded by class and cleanliness, with widths as expressions
  in inherited control count `c`; retained history is B × frame width for
  the full-history oracle expansion, otherwise the bound library's realised
  history (§17 item 18). Borrowed wires and scheme ancilla frames are
  separate records. Callees `qborrow` only from the caller's designated
  idle set. Shared frames require disjoint live ranges (suite F).

**Frame JSON schema (B5).** The descriptor is the following JCS object;
all five arrays are required, including empty arrays. Every record has
exactly the shown fields; `live_ranges` is optional. Unknown fields at
any depth are errors under §9.1.

```
  { "slots": [ { "id": "<slot identity>", "role": "in"|"tgt"|"fault"|"mut"|"mem_in"|"mem_mut", "class": "<cls>", "width": expr }, … ],
    "scratch": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "class": "<cls>", "state": "clean"|"dirty", "width": expr }, … ],
    "history": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "class": "<cls>", "width": expr }, … ],
    "borrow": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "class": "<cls>", "width": expr }, … ],
    "ancillae": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "scheme": "<scheme name>", "class": "<cls>", "state": "clean"|"dirty", "width": expr }, … ],
    "live_ranges"?: [ { "frame": "<frame identity>", "start": ordinal, "end": ordinal }, … ] }
```

`slots` lists the source interface in operand order: oracle `in`, `tgt`,
`fault`, then regions; action `mut`, `in`, then regions. Slot identities
are `<group>:<zero-based index within group>` (`in:0`, `tgt:0`,
`fault:0`, `mut:0`, `mem:0`, …); a region uses role `mem_in` or
`mem_mut`. A region slot's width counts all its cells, in address then
bit order; bundle bits are least-significant first. Slot widths MUST
match the signature. Inherited controls are caller-owned and precede
these slots in §11 topology placement; they are not renamed callee
slots. Every class is a resolved target class. Non-slot frame identities
are nonempty producer-chosen strings, distinct from every other record
identity in the descriptor; a dynamic identity additionally includes
the invocation context and the expansion scratch identity (§11.1).
Array order is source interface order for slots, and first allocation
order in the realised expansion for each other array. `scratch` records
per-class, per-cleanliness workspace; `history` records retained history;
`borrow` records dirty requirements; `ancillae` records each scheme's
ancilla frame and its declared state. These categories do not double
count the same wires. No requirements may be omitted because they vanish
for one chosen basis input.

Every `expr` uses §8.1's expression language with **only `c` bound**;
other variables and table lookups (whose indices require `k` or `eps`)
are invalid. At every covered inherited control count the widths MUST
be nonnegative integers; interface widths are positive and signature
fixed. Instantiation substitutes the resolved budget, parameters and
expansion choices into constants before forming these expressions.
Widths describe the actual realisation, including history and scheme
ancillae, and are checked against it. Each `live_ranges` entry names a
non-slot frame identity and a half-open interval `[start,end)` of its
invocation's generated leaf order (including step/phase/path expansion,
§11); `start < end`. A shared frame identity retains its recorded class, state and width
across uses; every use of shared storage MUST be listed and intervals
of the same frame MUST be pairwise disjoint. An omitted list gives no shared-storage permission.

**FRAME witness**

```json
{"ancillae":[{"class":"fast","id":"a","scheme":"controlled-x","state":"clean","width":{"var":"c"}}],"borrow":[{"class":"fast","id":"b","width":1}],"history":[{"class":"fast","id":"h","width":3}],"live_ranges":[{"end":2,"frame":"s","start":0},{"end":4,"frame":"s","start":2}],"scratch":[{"class":"fast","id":"s","state":"clean","width":{"args":[2,{"var":"c"}],"op":"+"}}],"slots":[{"class":"fast","id":"mut:0","role":"mut","width":2},{"class":"fast","id":"in:0","role":"in","width":1}]}
```

At `c = 2`, the slot widths total 3, scratch width is 4, retained
history width is 3, borrow width is 1 and scheme ancilla width is 2.
The two half-open uses of `s` meet at 2 and do not overlap. This is a

exec
